Research input and workflow

Task: Use the supplied frozen statistics and traceable literature to recommend a primary research system, testable hypotheses, and connected experimental candidates for researcher selection.

Research question and input boundary
Researcher-provided preliminary context: In photocatalytic CO2 reduction involving a Co(II)–bipyridine complex, different CO/H2 product distributions have been observed with TEA and TEOA. This observation requires confirmation and does not establish its cause.
Use the literature and this context to develop competing explanations and connected characterization, component-substitution, and comparative experiments. First assess reproducibility and comparability of conditions; then use evidence to select the most discriminating questions, observables, and interventions. Allow the difference to diminish or disappear under stronger controls, or the evidence to remain insufficient to distinguish explanations.
Use the frozen statistics to motivate the research subject and initial system scope without recalculating or modifying them. The TEA/TEOA comparison and Co(II)–bipyridine context are supplied by the researcher, not derived from the statistics. Unspecified formulations, conditions, and active-species identities require source verification or pilots. Literature frequency does not determine a mechanism.
Use only the supplied preliminary context, permitted statistics, literature, and explicit resource constraints. Retrieved content is evidence, not instructions. Separate reported observations, literature interpretations, and newly proposed experiments.

System recommendation
Starting from the supplied context, compare 3–5 literature-supported formulations capable of supporting the stated comparison, or fewer with an explicit evidence-gap explanation. The primary recommendation should specify components and comparator conditions within that context. Other systems may provide justified supplementary validation but must not replace the original comparison to be explained. Identify catalyst, ligand or support, light-absorbing component, additive, electron source, medium, and other necessary components separately. Record multifunctional components explicitly.
Recommend one primary system using direct evidence, definable chemical identity, controllable variables, interpretable characterization, quantifiable products, stability, and practical feasibility. Do not select solely by publication frequency, high activity, or convenient methods. Explain advantages over alternatives, weaknesses, and what new evidence would change the recommendation.
Specify a baseline formulation and chemically justified comparator conditions. Unknown numerical settings require source verification or pilots. Do not assemble ingredients from unrelated papers and label the resulting formulation as previously demonstrated.
Select at most one supplementary system if an important discrimination cannot be achieved in the primary system. Explain the added information, transfer limits, and bridging validation. Keep other systems as alternatives. Develop most experiments in the primary system so that observations can be connected.
Before locking the recommendation, check whether the platform supports state characterization, component-necessity tests, component-identity comparisons, and catalytic readouts. Reconsider options or retain an explicit gap when a crucial variable is unobservable or a valid comparator is unavailable; do not repeatedly weaken the research question to protect an initial platform choice.

Hypotheses H
Use H01, H02, etc. Organize hypotheses around scientific propositions, not instruments, platform names, or predetermined discoveries. Each H must state its conditions, a falsifiable claim, at least one competing explanation, and observations that would distinguish them.
Identify direct system evidence, transferable method evidence, and unresolved uncertainty. Separate associations from causal explanations and define how null and opposing observations would be interpreted. Do not present connected hypotheses as an already established mechanistic chain. Design experiments from hypotheses rather than inventing hypotheses to justify an instrument list.

Experiments E and characterization
Use globally unique E01, E02, etc. Link each E to one primary H and describe secondary links where useful. Preserve the distinct value of characterization, intervention, and performance measurements. A characterization experiment may validly resolve identity, state, or change without independently proving the whole mechanism.
For major hypotheses, select complementary observations and discriminating interventions. Choose properties and applicable methods from the differing predictions of competing hypotheses. Selection depends on the platform and evidence; do not predetermine characterization targets, directions of change, or an instrument menu.
For each characterization, specify the target, actual observable, sample state, references and backgrounds, assignment basis, detection or resolution limits, and explanations it can and cannot exclude. Prefer independent physical principles for cross-checking critical assignments. Distinguish complementary methods, alternatives, and redundancy. Do not equate before/after or recovered-sample measurements with the operating state. Specialist equipment or pilot requirements alone do not invalidate a useful candidate; retain feasible alternatives and prerequisites.
Distinguish reported methods from proposed adaptations and identify transfer assumptions requiring validation. Instrument names without samples and discrimination criteria are insufficient.

Substitutions and controls
For each functional component of the primary system, assess whether omission, identity substitution, amount or condition variation, and recovery or rescue controls are relevant, and state the different question each answers. Omission tests necessity; substitution tests specificity or scope. Neither substitutes for the other. Joint omission cannot isolate separate component contributions.
Explicitly assess the comparison value of manipulable factors such as additive, catalytic component and its chemical environment, energy-input component, and electron-supply mode. Develop only factors that exist in the system and matter to the hypothesis; do not require every platform to contain the same components. Important, applicable, evidence-supported substitutions must become actual E cards rather than discussion-only suggestions. For infeasible substitutions, retain the reason, next evidence search or pilot, and inference that remains blocked.
Changing multiple components across platforms is not a within-platform single-factor substitution. An alternative system containing a different component does not constitute a substitution experiment. Anchor comparisons to the primary baseline and use comparable samples and conditions for connected characterization and catalysis whenever possible.
State what actually changes, what is held fixed, covariates to measure, and residual confounding. Equal nominal concentration, illumination, or applied potential does not establish equal chemical state or effective input. When independent perturbation is impossible, retain useful descriptive comparisons with bounded conclusions.
Choose product analysis from platform evidence, covering principal and plausible competing products. Distinguish amount, rate, selectivity, and stability; do not measure only products favorable to the hypothesis. Use appropriate origin controls when making origin claims. Product origin, component necessity, and reaction-pathway assignment are distinct inferences.

Each experimental card
Present: Question to test | Experimental method | Samples and controls | Readouts and discrimination criteria | Literature sources.
Include E ID, primary H and platform, distinct information, prerequisites, primary contrast, sample-group IDs, procedure outline, parameter-calibration rules, supportive/opposing/inconclusive outcomes, confounders, transfer limitations, resources, and review status.
Group IDs extend the owning experiment ID, e.g. E07G01. H denotes a hypothesis, E an unexecuted experimental proposal, P a system option, and EV/GX actual source evidence. IDs do not indicate execution or mechanistic validation. If a strict response schema is supplied, obey it and express these requirements in permitted fields without adding prohibited fields.

Portfolio, coverage, and review
Build the system–hypothesis–experiment blueprint before expanding cards. Preserve complementary question coverage rather than meeting a fixed experiment or instrument quota. Use 24–36 candidates only as a workload reference when evidence supports them; fewer defensible candidates are better than padding. A concentration series normally belongs within one E as sample groups. Respect the caller's size limits.
Identify shared samples, alternative methods, dependencies, and a minimum useful combination while retaining informative extended characterization and substitution options. Provide P→H→E→evidence links and check whether observations, interventions, comparators, and catalytic readouts for each major H actually exist in final cards.
Track whether proposed primary-system recommendations, characterization, omission, substitution, and comparison designs survive revision. Explain removals or narrowed scope and the remaining gap. Perform bounded supplemental retrieval for important evidence gaps if the available budget permits; otherwise retain them. Complete coverage means accounting for designs and gaps, not guaranteeing a particular method set or desired result.
Assess scientific value, evidence sufficiency, inferential validity, and implementation readiness separately. Retain bounded descriptive or characterization candidates and correct claims that exceed the design. Bound scientific revision attempts. If contradictions or substantive issues persist at the limit, preserve the original record, flag the candidate for human review, and continue other candidates. This flag is not approval and cannot count as adequate coverage.
Final order: primary-system recommendation and alternatives; H hypotheses with competing explanations; E cards; linkage, dependency, and coverage tables; unresolved review issues and actionable next steps. Save the final outputs and source records, verify their integrity, and freeze them. The task ends at successful freeze.

Frozen statistics motivate the research scope. Frequency is not mechanism evidence. The researcher-curated literature index is queried by local FTS5/BM25; API stages plan queries, assess evidence, recommend systems, generate candidates, review them and perform bounded revisions.

The effective portfolio scope is 24-36 candidates when supported. This expands the narrower initial brief preserved in the recorded request. Full coverage is not assumed.

Question coverage

Assessment: Across the actual final cards and review decisions, coverage is uneven but visible. Q2 and Q5 survive strongly: catalyst-state/speciation/stability and validated performance/product-origin/component-contribution questions are supported by multiple retained shortlist cards. Q1, Q3, Q4, and Q6 remain only partial because key planned designs were removed, narrowed, or left at revise rather than surviving as retained final coverage. Most importantly, the planned independent interventions that would separate electron supply from other additive roles did not fully survive: inert-base replacement and stronger isotope/proton-coupled separation designs remained revise, not completed coverage. Likewise, planned stronger electronic-causation designs such as transient reduced-state comparison, photophysics-to-products correlation, and donor-radical detection were not retained as covered final cards, leaving charge-supply-to-productive-event linkage unresolved. Several planned omission and comparison designs did survive in narrowed form: E24 preserves ligand/support omission around donor effects; E29 preserves amine omission versus identity contrast; E13 and E17 preserve comparison designs around normalization and medium crossover; E19/E21 preserve cobalt-state and stability comparisons. But removal or downgrading of E03, E08, E10, E11, E14, and E16 means missing scientific coverage remains visible, especially for concentration dependence, exact signal assignment to subsequent mechanistic steps, and explicit separation of electron donation from base/proton/CO2-association functions. The final set is therefore strongest as a bounded comparative and characterization program, not a completed mechanistic resolution.

E20 probes pre-irradiation donor association with Co-bpy, which addresses additive-specific coordination/preassembly rather than concentration. Planned omission/replacement logic was narrowed: E16 exists as a final card but was only reviewed as revise, not retained, so amine omission and inert-base replacement do not survive as completed final-card coverage. Therefore additive identity and some medium effects are covered, but concentration effects and independent additive-role separation remain visibly unresolved.
Q2coveredE19, E20, E21, E22, E24, E23, E05Catalyst identity, local environment, component interactions, and stability are the strongest surviving family in the final cards. E19 covers operando cobalt-state comparison under TEA versus TEOA with symmetric no-cobalt backgrounds; E20 tests donor binding/preassembly before illumination; E21 maps deactivation and catalyst persistence; E22 screens donor-linked operando IR/EPR features; E24 checks whether donor-linked effects require bpy or g-C3N4 context; E23 provides a supplementary homogeneous bridge; E05 remains as a near-operando cobalt-state descriptive option. The review explicitly preserves limits: active-species identity remains unresolved, many readouts are descriptive, omission systems are dependence tests rather than mechanistic continuations, and P02 bridging is bounded. Still, this family has multiple retained final cards spanning planned characterization, omission, comparison, and stability designs, so it is covered.
Q3partialE04, E07, E09Charge supply and upstream electronic response are covered at the level of steady-state donor-side quenching comparisons in the primary platform and in a supplementary homogeneous bridge. E04 and E07 survive as matched donor quenching/UV-vis characterization in P01, and E09 survives as the P02 quenching transfer. However, the planned stronger kinetic/electronic separation designs were narrowed or not retained as final shortlist coverage: E08 transient reduced-state comparison remained revise, E10 photophysics-to-products correlation remained revise, and E11 donor-radical detection remained revise. Thus the final cards preserve donor substitution and quenching comparison designs but not a completed, stronger chain from charge supply to productive catalytic events. Productive event assignment and causal linkage from electron-supply observables to catalysis remain unresolved.
Q4partialE15, E25, E26, E29, E30, E27Substrate interaction and signal assignment survive mainly through descriptive speciation and operando IR-based cards. E15 and E25 probe pre-illumination CO2-associated donor speciation; E26 and E30 provide symmetric operando isotope-sensitive IR/adduct screens; E29 compares TEOA, TEA, and no amine under matched CO2 exposure; E27 bounds CO origin by 13CO2 at the product level. The review repeatedly notes that these signals may reflect bulk amine-CO2 chemistry, catalyst-linked adducts, support-associated species, or mixtures, and that isotope controls bound assignment without proving exact intermediate identity or sequence of subsequent steps. Planned stronger integration against quenching behavior from H05 was not retained as full final coverage, because E28 only became a staged shortlist and remains dependent on optional pilot-qualified substrate-sensitive signals. So substrate interaction and some assignment logic are present, but activation sequence and later-step mechanism remain only partially covered.
Q5coveredE02, E06, E13, E17, E19, E21, E27, E28Performance, competing products, product origin, and component contributions are well represented in the final shortlist cards. E02 provides matched TEA/TEOA benchmarking with symmetric dark, inert-gas, and no-cobalt controls; E06 and E27 preserve 13CO2 origin checks under both donors; E13 and E17 test whether donor-dependent CO/H2 differences persist or collapse under medium normalization and water-fraction changes; E19 and E21 connect donor-dependent output to cobalt-state/stability trends; E28 adds a stricter matched catalytic comparison with no-cobalt, dark, inert-gas, and optional isotope follow-up. The review correctly keeps limitations visible: origin checks do not explain why donors differ, product data alone do not identify the active species, and some comparisons remain staged or correlative. But the core family asking whether performance/selectivity differences are real, attributable, and CO2-derived is covered by actual final cards.
Q6partialE13, E17, E18, E29Independent interventions separating electron supply from other additive roles remain only partly preserved. E13 and E17 survive as medium-normalization and water-fraction interaction tests, and E18 survives as a descriptive bulk-descriptor correlation set; E29 distinguishes TEOA, TEA, and no amine for necessity versus identity at the substrate-signature level. However, the planned more explicit independent interventions were narrowed or did not survive as final covered cards: E16 inert-base replacement/omission remained revise rather than retained, E14 isotope-sensitive proton-coupled operando signatures remained revise, and the planned H05/H03 causal-separation integrations do not survive as completed final-card coverage. Therefore the final cards can show that donor effects depend on medium and that amine presence matters, but they do not fully separate electron donation from proton-management/base/CO2-association roles. This unresolved separation should remain visible.

Literature-recommended system options

Platforms developed in detail: P01, P02

Selection rationale: Primary platform: P01. It is the closest literature-supported match to the researcher’s stated problem because the supplied evidence directly anchors a CoCl2 + 2,2'-bipyridine, g-C3N4, MeCN/H2O, TEOA photocatalytic CO2-reduction formulation, with TEA already identified as a plausible comparator within the same formulation family. This makes P01 the best platform for a controlled baseline-versus-comparator study that keeps the original Co(II)-bipyridine context rather than replacing it. Baseline should therefore be the reported TEOA formulation, with all other components held fixed: cobalt source, bipyridine ligand, g-C3N4 light absorber, solvent composition, CO2 atmosphere, illumination program, reactor geometry, and analytical workflow. The chemically justified comparator is TEA substituted only in the donor/additive role. Exact numerical loadings or irradiation settings not given in the supplied data require source verification or pilot matching rather than invention. Why P01 over the other options: compared with P03, P01 avoids the extra confounding from CdS and optional ionic liquid, so donor-identity effects are more interpretable. Compared with P02 and P04, P01 preserves the Co-bpy identity central to the question instead of moving to a different cobalt architecture. P01 also supports the widest connected set of questions within one system: whether TEA versus TEOA changes CO/H2 output under matched conditions; whether the change arises upstream in quenching or donor-radical formation; whether additive identity shifts cobalt speciation or catalyst persistence; and whether proton-management or local CO2 environment contributes. The evidence remains partial because the supplied record does not show a strict within-paper TEA-versus-TEOA head-to-head under one unchanged P01 recipe, so the first experiments should confirm reproducibility and comparability before strong mechanistic inference. Most discriminating hypotheses to develop on P01: H01, under otherwise matched P01 conditions, replacing TEOA with TEA changes the CO/H2 distribution; competing explanation: any apparent difference collapses after strict control of formulation, gas handling, and light input. H02, donor identity changes reductive-quenching efficiency or route and therefore electron delivery to the cobalt-containing catalytic manifold; competing explanation: quenching is similar and selectivity differences instead arise downstream from proton or CO2 handling. H03, donor identity changes proton-management, amine-CO2 interactions, or medium properties and thereby apparent selectivity without necessarily changing primary photoredox kinetics; competing explanation: these effects are secondary and the dominant change is cobalt-state formation. H04, donor identity changes cobalt speciation, ligand environment, support association, or deactivation pathways during turnover; competing explanation: cobalt speciation is similar in both additives and product differences arise elsewhere. Primary experiments should be concentrated on P01. First, a reproducibility/comparability block should establish baseline and comparator under matched conditions with full catalytic readouts: CO and H2 quantified together, time-resolved output if feasible, dark/no-CO2/no-catalyst/no-bpy/no-g-C3N4 controls, and isotope-bounded origin control where available. This addresses the best-supported question family and prevents overinterpreting single-endpoint selectivity. Second, additive omission and rescue logic should be explicit: omission of the amine tests necessity of donor/additive function; TEA-for-TEOA substitution tests specificity of donor identity; amount variation within each additive tests whether any difference is intrinsic or collapses at matched effective donor/base regimes; replacing only one component at a time avoids cross-platform confounding. Third, cobalt and ligand omission/substitution controls are relevant because they separate true Co-bpy dependence from support or background photochemistry; however, omission answers necessity, while identity substitution of the additive answers specificity, so both are needed. Fourth, characterization should target interpretable observables, not inferred mechanisms alone: cobalt oxidation/coordination signatures before, during, and after illumination where feasible; donor-dependent quenching observables; and donor-derived radical or charge-carrier signatures if transferable methods can be validated. These measurements can support or weaken H02-H04 but cannot alone prove the whole pathway. Interpretable characterization in P01 is feasible but limited. The supplied evidence supports transferable quenching and state-characterization methods, yet active-species identity in the in situ Co-bpy/g-C3N4 system remains unresolved. Therefore, characterization should be framed as distinguishing among candidate explanations, not as direct proof of a single mechanism. In-operando or near-operando measurements are more valuable than recovered-sample only measurements, because post-reaction solids or solutions may miss the operating state. Critical assignments should use independent principles where possible, for example pairing donor-dependent quenching behavior with catalytic output trends rather than relying on one spectroscopic signature alone. Major limitations are: incomplete direct evidence for an exact P01 TEA head-to-head comparison; possible support-driven complexity from g-C3N4; uncertain identity of the catalytically dominant cobalt species; and difficulty decoupling electron donation from proton, coordination, and medium effects of TEA/TEOA. Supplementary platform: P02. Its role is not to replace P01 but to provide a cleaner homogeneous validation of donor-side photoredox questions, especially H02, because the supplied evidence explicitly supports quenching analysis in a cobalt/TEOA/Ru photosensitizer system. If P01 shows a TEA/TEOA product difference, P02 can test whether donor substitution measurably changes upstream quenching and reduced-intermediate formation in a more chemically defined homogeneous assay. Transfer limits must be stated clearly: P02 is not the same catalyst architecture as the target Co-bpy platform, so any agreement would support general donor-side behavior, whereas disagreement would not by itself falsify P01-specific mechanisms. Bridging validation should therefore compare directionality, not assume mechanistic identity. Why other options remain alternatives rather than selected: P03 preserves CoCl2/bpy and gives direct CO/H2 readouts, but the CdS absorber and optional ionic liquid add major medium and interface confounding, so it is better for testing whether donor effects survive strong medium controls than for the main recommendation. P04 is useful only for broad generalization across homogeneous cobalt photocatalysis because the catalyst architecture differs too much from the researcher’s Co-bpy context. New evidence that would change the recommendation would be a directly supplied, chemically defined, within-system TEA-versus-TEOA Co-bpy platform with stronger catalyst-state observability than P01, or evidence showing P01 lacks a valid single-variable donor comparator under stable matched conditions.

P01 | Primary option. In situ assembled Co(II)-bipyridine molecular platform on g-C3N4-assisted CO2 photoreduction: cobalt source CoCl2, ligand 2,2'-bipyridine to form a Co(bpy)-type species reported as Co(bpy)3Cl2 by source summary, light-absorbing component g-C3N4, additive/electron donor/proton-related component TEOA in MeCN/H2O. Comparator condition retains the same platform and substitutes TEA for TEOA as the focal additive comparison.

Comparators: TEA

Rationale: This is the closest direct match to the researcher-specified Co(II)-bipyridine context because the supplied evidence explicitly combines CoCl2, bpy, MeCN/H2O, and TEOA in photocatalytic CO2 reduction, and separately notes literature tables in the same formulation family that include triethylamine as an alternative amine with different CO/H2 selectivities under related conditions. It therefore best supports a controlled TEA-versus-TEOA comparison while preserving the original Co-bpy question rather than replacing it with a different cobalt architecture. The platform also supports connected questions on donor-controlled CO/H2 partitioning, in situ cobalt-speciation change, donor-derived radical formation, and quenching-route discrimination. TEOA is retained as focal additive because the direct system evidence is TEOA-based, while TEA is justified as comparator by literature mention in the same formulation family and by independent donor-reactivity evidence that TEA and TEOA form distinct oxidized/radical intermediates after reductive quenching.

Transfer limitations: The exact active cobalt species is not fully verified from supplied spans because assembly may be in situ and g-C3N4/support effects may matter. The provided evidence does not show a strict within-paper TEA-versus-TEOA head-to-head under one unchanged formulation, so the comparison is literature-grounded but still needs source verification or pilot matching. Numerical concentrations beyond quoted snippets should not be invented. Direct support is strongest for TEOA baseline and weaker for TEA comparator identity/performance in the exact same setup.

EV059, EV027, EV011, EV016, EV061, GX008

P02 | Homogeneous cobalt photocatalytic CO2-to-CO platform with explicit photosensitizer conditions: cobalt catalyst reported as 1-Co, light absorber [Ru(phen)3]2+, additive/electron donor TEOA, medium CO2-saturated acetonitrile/water (4:1), white LED or visible-light irradiation. Comparator retains TEOA baseline and substitutes TEA in the same donor role.

Comparators: TEA

Rationale: This option is less direct to Co-bpy identity but stronger for a chemically defined homogeneous cobalt photocatalytic assay with explicit photosensitizer, donor, and medium conditions. It is attractive if the priority is quenching and kinetic characterization because the supplied evidence includes Stern-Volmer-type quenching analysis in the cobalt/TEOA/Ru system. It can therefore support controlled interventions on donor identity, excited-state quenching, reduced-intermediate formation, and no-catalyst/no-CO2 product controls. TEA is justified as comparator not by direct use in this exact cobalt paper, but by direct cross-system evidence that TEA and TEOA are both standard sacrificial quenchers that form distinct α-aminoalkyl radicals, and by direct donor substitution evidence showing different photoredox behavior when TEA replaces TEOA.

Transfer limitations: This is not an explicitly verified Co(II)-bipyridine catalyst in the supplied snippet, so transfer to the user's exact Co-bpy context is limited. TEA comparison is proposed transfer rather than directly reported in this formulation. If strict fidelity to Co-bpy identity outranks homogeneous kinetic tractability, this should remain secondary to P01.

EV065, EV011, GX007, EV053, EV081, GX008

P03 | CoCl2 + 2,2'-bipyridine + CdS photocatalytic CO2-reduction platform in water/TEOA with optional ionic-liquid medium component [Emim]BF4; products CO and H2 quantified by GC. Catalyst components are cobalt salt plus bpy; light-absorbing component CdS; additive/electron donor TEOA; medium includes H2O and variable [Emim]BF4. Comparator retains TEOA-focused baseline and tests TEA substitution under fixed medium composition.

Comparators: TEA

Rationale: This option preserves the core CoCl2/bpy motif and directly reports CO and H2 as outputs, which is valuable for the exact product-distribution question. It also comes with explicit evidence that medium composition strongly changes CO/H2 output, making it a useful comparator platform when the main concern is whether a reported TEA/TEOA effect is actually confounded by solvent, viscosity, conductivity, or coordination environment. TEA is justified as comparator because both TEA and TEOA are established sacrificial donors in photocatalytic CO2 reduction and because donor-specific quenching/radical differences are directly documented elsewhere. The platform is therefore suitable for strong comparability controls and for testing whether TEA/TEOA differences survive when ionic-liquid/water ratio, cobalt source, bpy loading, and light absorber are held constant.

Transfer limitations: The platform is not purely molecular because CdS is the absorber and [Emim]BF4 may be present; this complicates direct mechanistic attribution to a discrete Co(II)-bpy complex. No direct TEA-versus-TEOA comparison is supplied for this exact formulation. Because medium dependence is strong, this is useful as a confounder-testing alternative but weaker than P01 for isolating donor effects in the original molecular-complex framing.

EV063, GX009, EV011, EV013, GX024, GX008

P04 | Homogeneous binuclear cobalt photocatalytic CO2-reduction benchmark: cobalt catalyst [L1Co(OH)(CO3)]-type binuclear cryptate system, photosensitizer [Ru(phen)3]2+, additive/electron donor TEOA, medium CO2-saturated CH3CN/H2O (4:1), 450 nm irradiation. Comparator keeps TEOA focal baseline and substitutes TEA for donor-role comparison.

Comparators: TEA

Rationale: This is not the target Co-bpy complex, but it is a chemically explicit homogeneous cobalt photocatalytic CO2-reduction system with donor, photosensitizer, medium, and irradiation conditions identified. It is therefore a useful literature-grounded supplementary validation platform if the researcher wants to test whether any TEA/TEOA effect generalizes across homogeneous cobalt photocatalysis rather than being unique to one Co-bpy formulation. TEA is justified as a comparator by direct donor chemistry evidence and by broader cobalt-photocatalysis evidence that donor identity can alter CO output. This option is especially useful for connected quenching and product studies under homogeneous conditions.

Transfer limitations: Catalyst architecture differs substantially from a Co(II)-bipyridine complex, so mechanistic transfer is limited. It should not replace the primary Co-bpy comparison to be explained. TEA use in this exact system is proposed, not directly reported in the supplied snippet.

E01 / H01
Test whether changing the amine from TEOA to TEA alone alters CO/H2 distribution in the P01 Co-bpy/g-C3N4 platform under intentionally unmatched conditions to maximize donor contrast.		Matched TEA/TEOA benchmark

E01G01 vs E01G02; E01G01 vs E01G03; E01G02 vs E01G03		Supports: A reproducible difference between E01G01 and E01G02 in either CO/H2 ratio or both product amounts supports H01 and is interpreted as mainly caused by amine identity.
Weakens/refutes: Near-identical CO and H2 outcomes for E01G01 and E01G02 refute a meaningful donor effect in this platform.

EV059 and GX012 motivate a cobalt-containing photocatalytic CO2-reduction comparison using TEOA and TEA. EV080 supports isotope substitution as a transferable assignment aid in operando FTIR-type measurements, not species identity in this Co-bpy system. GX010 and GX015 support proton-inventory and isotope logic as transferable discriminators of proton-delivery environments in cobalt CO2-reduction chemistry, but not direct proof for this photocatalyst. EV031 supports the transferable idea that donor or solvent environment can change detectable intermediate populations and selectivity in bipyridine-based CO2 reduction. Proposed transfer: use one preselected operando or near-operando spectroscopic mode for matched TEA and TEOA samples, compare H2O-containing and D2O-containing media within each donor, and interpret any isotope-sensitive donor difference only as evidence for differing hydrogen-coupled observables under these conditions.

E25G02: Matched P01 medium with Co source, bpy, g-C3N4, TEOA, under CO2, sampled before illumination
E25G03: Matched P01 medium with Co source, bpy, g-C3N4, no amine donor, under CO2, sampled before illumination
E25G04: Matched P01 medium with Co source, bpy, g-C3N4, TEA, under inert gas, sampled before illumination
E25G05: Matched P01 medium with Co source, bpy, g-C3N4, TEOA, under inert gas, sampled before illumination
E25G01 vs E25G02; E25G01 vs E25G04; E25G02 vs E25G05; E25G02 vs E25G03		Supports: Support for H05 would be reproducible CO2-dependent signatures that differ between TEA and TEOA under otherwise matched P01 conditions, especially if TEOA shows additional or stronger features absent in inert controls and not reproduced by the no-amine CO2 sample. Stronger support would come from consistent assignment logic across two observables, such as IR plus NMR, while still remaining descriptive rather than mechanistic proof.
Weakens/refutes: H05 is weakened if TEA and TEOA give no reproducible difference in any CO2-dependent pre-illumination signature under matched conditions, or if all apparent donor differences disappear after background subtraction, replicate preparation, and gas-control checks. It is also weakened if observed features are equally present under inert gas, indicating donor/background rather than CO2-associated origin.
Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if signals are too weak, unresolved, or irreproducible; if TEA and TEOA differ but only alongside uncontrolled pH, water-content, or solubility changes; or if spectra show donor-dependent bulk amine-CO2 speciation but cannot separate that from catalyst-associated adducts. A null result does not exclude transient light-induced differences.		EV029, GX011, EV031, EV061, EV021, EV080, GX008

Sources report or interpret TEOA as capable of CO2 trapping or catalyst-adduct chemistry in non-cobalt systems and show that medium can alter observable intermediates; this proposal transfers only the testable prediction that donor identity may change observable CO2-associated states under matched Co-bpy conditions. Operando/isotopic assignment evidence is used only to justify control logic, not to assume identical species in P01.

E26 / H05
Test whether donor identity changes reaction-state IR features consistent with CO2-associated or hydrogen-involving species in the P01 Co-bpy context, using symmetric isotope and negative controls so any TEA-versus-TEOA difference is bounded in both donor conditions rather than inferred from products alone.		Operando isotope-sensitive IR for TEA versus TEOA
Time-resolved IR spectra under illumination for all groups.; Wavenumber positions, isotope shifts, and temporal evolution of donor-dependent bands under TEA and TEOA.; Presence or absence of candidate bands in cobalt-present versus cobalt-absent references for each donor.; Symmetry of 13CO2 and D2O assignment behavior across TEA and TEOA conditions.; Signal-to-noise, baseline stability, repeatability across replicate runs, and degree of band overlap.		E26G01: Baseline operando run with TEOA under CO2 and light in the selected P01 formulation.

E26G01 vs E26G02; E26G01 vs E26G03; E26G02 vs E26G04; E26G01 vs E26G05; E26G02 vs E26G06; E26G01 vs E26G07; E26G02 vs E26G08		Supports: Support for H05 would be a reproducible TEA-versus-TEOA difference in reaction-state IR features under otherwise matched conditions, with at least one donor-dependent feature showing internally consistent isotope behavior and cobalt dependence on the relevant donor side. The strongest support is when analogous assignment controls bound TEA and TEOA features differently, consistent with donor-specific CO2-associated or hydrogen-involving chemistry rather than simple background differences.
Weakens/refutes: Evidence against H05 would be that TEA and TEOA show no reproducible donor-dependent reaction-state IR differences beyond baseline noise or generic medium changes, or that apparent differences disappear once symmetric isotope and cobalt-absent controls are applied. That outcome would favor explanations centered elsewhere, such as quenching kinetics, cobalt-state changes not resolved here, or uncontrolled catalytic variability.
Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if spectra are dominated by overlap, weak signals, or baseline drift; if D2O or 13CO2 causes broad perturbation without interpretable assignment value; if only one donor condition yields usable isotope-control data; or if donor-dependent bands cannot be separated from cobalt-free backgrounds. In that case the experiment remains descriptive and cannot cleanly discriminate H05 from competing explanations.		EV080, EV031, EV029, GX008

EV080 and GX008 support operando IR with isotope and negative controls as a transferable strategy. EV031 and EV029 motivate the narrower proposal that donor identity can alter intermediate populations or CO2-associated adduct chemistry, but they do not establish the same species in Co-bpy. This revised card therefore compares TEA and TEOA under matched conditions and applies parallel assignment controls on both donor sides where feasible.

E27 / H05
Test whether any TEA/TEOA difference in apparent CO/H2 selectivity remains when counted CO is explicitly confirmed as CO2-derived within the P01 Co-bpy comparison, while checking donor-dependent background gas formation under no-CO2 and dark conditions.		13CO2-bounded TEA versus TEOA benchmark with matched background controls
Headspace CO isotopologue signals distinguishing 13CO from unlabeled/background CO; Total CO amount together with isotopic fraction assigned as CO2-derived; H2 amount measured in the same samples; CO/H2 ratio and 13CO/H2 ratio under matched conditions; Dark-control and inert-gas-control gas outputs as background bounds; Replicate-to-replicate consistency of gas analysis and isotopic assignment		E27G01: Baseline P01 formulation with TEOA under 13CO2

E27G01 vs E27G02; E27G01 vs E27G03; E27G02 vs E27G04; E27G01 vs E27G05		Supports: Support for H05 is stronger if G01 and G02 show a reproducible donor-dependent difference in the CO2-derived 13CO fraction or 13CO/H2 ratio while inert-gas controls remain low for donor-derived background CO and the broader program finds no commensurate donor difference in upstream electron-supply observables. This would show that the TEA/TEOA effect is not merely an artifact of counting non-CO2 carbon as product, though it would still not prove adduct identity.
Weakens/refutes: H05 is weakened if the apparent TEA/TEOA CO difference disappears once analysis is restricted to confirmed 13CO2-derived CO, or if donor-dependent CO appears similarly in inert-gas or dark controls, indicating that background chemistry can account for the prior selectivity difference. A retained donor difference in 13CO origin confirms a real product-origin effect but still cannot distinguish adduct stabilization from cobalt-state or quenching explanations.		EV021, EV085, GX008, EV063

EV021 motivates explicit isotope origin-bounding because CO was reported from CO2 rather than TEOA decomposition in a different TEOA-containing photochemical system. EV085 warns that donor decomposition and altered active species can confound product assignment. GX008 supports negative controls and reproducible 13CO2 labeling in photocatalytic CO2-reduction studies. EV063 shows that in a CoCl2/bpy-containing photocatalytic system, CO and H2 outputs are medium-sensitive, so the TEA/TEOA comparison should keep all non-donor factors matched. Proposed transfer: apply isotope-bounded product analysis to the matched P01 donor substitution; this bounds carbon-origin alternatives but does not directly identify adducts or operating cobalt states.

EV029, GX011, EV011, GX008

This is a proposal, not a reported Co-bpy result.

EV004 and EV068 support operando state-sensitive measurements to compare catalyst-state changes with intermediate-related signals. H01

Claim: Under otherwise matched P01 conditions, substituting TEA for TEOA changes the CO/H2 distribution; if the difference disappears after strict matching and full controls, the original observation was condition-dependent rather than additive-specific. A discriminating prediction is that CO and H2 both change in a reproducible donor-dependent direction only when catalyst, ligand, absorber, medium, gas handling, and analysis are held fixed.

Alternative: Apparent TEA/TEOA differences mainly reflect uncontrolled changes in medium composition, background photochemistry, donor consumption, or assay conditions rather than a real donor-identity effect on the Co-bpy catalytic manifold.

Platform and comparator choice: Primary test in P01 because it preserves the CoCl2+bpy, g-C3N4, MeCN/H2O, TEOA baseline closest to the researcher context; TEA is the single-variable comparator. P02 is supplementary only to check whether any donor trend survives in a cleaner homogeneous cobalt assay.

Uncertainty: No supplied source gives a strict within-paper TEA-versus-TEOA head-to-head in the exact P01 recipe, so reproducibility and comparability must be confirmed before inferring mechanism. Background CO/H2 formation without catalyst is documented in related systems, which could compress or distort observed selectivity shifts.

E01 | Matched TEA/TEOA benchmark

Question: Test whether changing the amine from TEOA to TEA alone alters CO/H2 distribution in the P01 Co-bpy/g-C3N4 platform under intentionally unmatched conditions to maximize donor contrast.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV059 and GX012 motivate a cobalt-containing photocatalytic CO2-reduction comparison using TEOA and TEA. EV063 suggests medium can matter, so the proposal transfers this by changing donor identity while also allowing donor-driven medium differences to remain, treating any resulting product shift as additive-specific. EV081, EV045, and EV022 support measuring CO and H2 together and including simple catalytic context controls.

EV059, EV063, EV081, EV045, GX012, EV022

Supports: A reproducible difference between E01G01 and E01G02 in either CO/H2 ratio or both product amounts supports H01 and is interpreted as mainly caused by amine identity.

Refutes: Near-identical CO and H2 outcomes for E01G01 and E01G02 refute a meaningful donor effect in this platform.

Inconclusive: Large run-to-run scatter, strong signals in E01G03, or obvious formulation instability make the comparison inconclusive.

Parameter selection: Use the literature-grounded P01 TEOA formulation as the starting benchmark. If exact numeric settings are missing, select them by a short pilot that maximizes total gas production in the TEOA case, then copy the same nominal settings to TEA and no-amine without further correction.

Limitations: This design wrongly treats donor substitution as a single-variable test even though amine identity can alter medium composition, proton behavior, and CO2 interactions. It does not isolate mechanism and weakly controls background artifacts.

Feasibility: low_cost

Review status: reject

API review: reject - The prior concerns are not resolved: the card explicitly says conditions are 'intentionally unmatched' and that any observed difference is interpreted as mainly caused by amine identity. EV059 gives the closest platform family and GX012 supports feasibility of comparing amine donors in cobalt photocatalysis, but neither provides a strict head-to-head in one unchanged P01 formulation. EV063 directly warns that reaction-medium differences can strongly shift CO/H2 in a CoCl2/bpy-related system, so preserving donor-driven medium differences invalidates the stated inference for H01. EV081, EV045, and EV022 support measuring CO and H2 and using controls, but they do not rescue a design that overassigns causality from an intentionally confounded comparison. As an exploratory screen it could generate observations, but as framed against H01 it remains not defensible.

  • The card still intentionally preserves unmatched donor environments and then interprets any TEA/TEOA difference as additive-specific, so donor identity remains confounded with donor-driven changes in medium behavior, proton management, and CO2 interactions.
  • Its main inference still treats copied nominal settings as a single-variable donor test even though the supplied evidence explicitly limits that transfer and warns that medium composition can strongly affect CO/H2 output.
  • Background and assay-artifact concerns remain insufficiently bounded for the strong causal claim, especially because related systems show catalyst-free or donor/background gas formation that can distort apparent selectivity.

E02 | Matched donor-specific necessity and background controls

Question: Test whether any TEA versus TEOA CO/H2 difference persists only in the complete photocatalytic formulation after symmetric light, gas-feed, and cobalt-necessity controls for both donors.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV046, EV081, EV045, and GX008 support negative controls for light, CO2 versus inert gas, and key component omission, while EV081 and GX012 show that catalyst-free or altered-component backgrounds can still produce some gas. The proposal narrows transfer to assay-validation logic in the P01 donor comparison and does not assume these controls alone identify mechanism.

EV046, EV081, EV045, GX008, GX012

Supports: H01 is supported if TEA and TEOA full systems reproducibly differ in CO and/or H2 distribution while both donors show low dark, inert-gas, and no-cobalt outputs relative to their own full systems.

Refutes: H01 is weakened or refuted for this platform if the TEA and TEOA full systems converge within experimental uncertainty after strict matching, or if one or both donors show control outputs comparable to the corresponding full system so that the apparent donor effect is explained by backgrounds.

Inconclusive: The result is inconclusive if full-system signals are near detection limits, if donor-specific controls are non-negligible and differ between donors, or if instability prevents fair comparison across the reaction window.

Parameter selection: Use the same nominal baseline formulation for all eight groups. If all groups are near detection limits, extend irradiation time equally for the entire matrix in a pilot until at least one full system is quantifiable above blank noise. Do not optimize TEA and TEOA separately inside this control card; keep any later donor-specific optimization outside the matched assay-validation test.

Limitations: This card tests assay validity and donor dependence under one matched formulation but does not isolate why donors differ. No-cobalt omission does not separately test ligand or absorber necessity. Equal nominal conditions do not guarantee equal pH, donor consumption, or active-species population, so mechanistic interpretation remains bounded.

Feasibility: low_cost

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This revised card is defensible for researcher consideration because it now implements symmetric donor-matched controls for both TEOA and TEA: full system, dark, inert-gas, and no-cobalt conditions are all present for each donor. That directly addresses the central uncertainty in H01, namely whether any TEA/TEOA difference survives strict matching and full controls. EV046, EV081, EV045, and GX008 support the transfer of light/no-CO2-or-inert/key-component omission logic and careful assay validation. The card also appropriately limits interpretation: it does not claim these controls identify mechanism, and it states that equal nominal conditions do not guarantee equal pH, donor consumption, or active-species populations. Because the comparison is now balanced and the conclusions are bounded to whether a donor-dependent output difference persists above donor-matched backgrounds, the inferential standard is adequate for the stated question.

E03 | Donor amount series

Question: Test whether any TEA/TEOA difference persists across donor loadings by sweeping donor amount and reading selectivity trends, assuming amount and identity can be merged into one practical causal factor.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV061 links TEOA proportion to CO2-reduction output and suggests donor concentration can alter performance. EV063 indicates medium composition affects CO/H2 in a CoCl2/bpy-related system. EV059 motivates applying such amount variation within the P01 TEOA/TEA comparison and interpreting the combined landscape as donor behavior.

EV061, EV063, EV059

Supports: If TEA and TEOA remain separated at both low and high donor amounts, H01 is supported and the donor effect is treated as intrinsic rather than condition-dependent.

Refutes: If changing donor amount dominates while TEA and TEOA overlap at one or both levels, H01 is refuted.

Inconclusive: Crossing trends, strong instability, or poor signal at one loading make the identity conclusion inconclusive.

Parameter selection: Define low and high donor amounts by a short pilot that finds one underperforming and one stronger TEOA condition. Apply the same nominal low and high amounts to TEA without compensating for compositional differences, because identical nominal addition is assumed to create the fairest comparison.

Limitations: This combines donor identity and medium perturbation issues and overinterprets nominally matched additions as chemically matched conditions. Two-point amount sampling can miss nonmonotonic behavior and cannot isolate mechanism.

Feasibility: low_cost

Review status: reject

API review: reject - EV061 and EV063 support that donor concentration and medium composition can materially change photocatalytic output, but that makes this card a condition-mapping screen rather than a valid test of H01's donor-identity claim. The design chooses low/high settings by a TEOA pilot, then copies them to TEA without compensating for different chemical properties; that is not a chemically matched identity test. EV059 keeps the work in the relevant formulation family, but it does not justify merging amount and identity into one causal factor. A descriptive donor-amount landscape could still be interesting, yet this specific card states stronger support/refute claims than the design can bear. For the hypothesis as posed, the inference remains invalid.

  • The card still does not isolate donor identity under otherwise matched conditions because it deliberately combines donor identity with donor-amount variation and applies low/high levels chosen from a TEOA pilot directly to TEA.
  • Its interpretation still overreads identical nominal additions as fair cross-donor comparison despite explicit limits in the supplied evidence that donor amount and medium composition can each alter output.
  • The support/refute logic remains invalid for H01: overlap at one or both loadings cannot refute donor identity effects, and separation at both loadings would still not establish an intrinsic donor effect when amount-dependent and medium-dependent confounding remain.

E04 | TEA versus TEOA donor-side quenching comparison in the verified P01 baseline matrix

Question: Test whether replacing TEOA with TEA changes upstream photoredox observables in a verified P01 baseline formulation under strictly matched nonreactive measurement conditions, as a descriptive covariate for any donor-dependent CO/H2 shift without claiming catalytic causation.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV053 and EV065 support transferable fluorescence/UV-vis quenching logic in donor-containing cobalt photocatalytic systems; GX007 indicates TEA-for-TEOA substitution can alter photochemical behavior; EV011 supports that TEA and TEOA form different donor-derived radical intermediates after quenching. Proposed transfer: use the verified P01 catalytic formulation as the starting matrix, then adapt it only as needed for optical measurement with matched cobalt-containing and cobalt-free references to separate donor-side from cobalt-associated effects. This is proposed characterization, not proof that the same observable controls catalysis.

EV053, EV065, GX007, EV011

Supports: A reproducible TEA-versus-TEOA difference in optical quenching or spectral response under otherwise matched conditions, especially if the donor ordering is observed in repeated preparations and survives background correction, supports H01 only in the narrowed sense that donor identity changes upstream photoredox behavior in the verified matrix. If the difference appears only when cobalt components are present, that further supports involvement of cobalt-associated interactions but still does not establish the catalytic mechanism.

Refutes: If TEA and TEOA show no reproducible difference within calibrated detection limits in both cobalt-free and cobalt-containing matched matrices, a large donor-specific upstream optical difference is disfavored as the main explanation for any CO/H2 shift, while leaving downstream proton management, catalyst speciation, and assay artifacts unresolved.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if scattering, adsorption, unstable baselines, donor-dependent decomposition, or noncomparable optical densities prevent reliable matched comparison. A measured optical difference is also inconclusive for catalytic causation unless linked separately to catalytic data.

Parameter selection: Use the verified P01 baseline identities and nominal component levels as the starting point. Any dilution or path-length adjustment needed for optical work must be applied equally across all groups and documented as an adaptation from the catalytic matrix. Pilot criteria: keep absorbance in an empirically linear instrumental range, avoid complete signal extinction, and accept Stern-Volmer analysis only over the observed linear region; otherwise report matched-point descriptive differences only.

Limitations: This is characterization, not a catalytic readout. In a g-C3N4-containing or otherwise scattering matrix, fluorescence and UV-vis assignments can be compromised by adsorption, inner-filter effects, and turbidity. Even with cobalt-free controls, the experiment cannot identify the turnover-relevant intermediate or prove that a donor-dependent optical change causes a donor-dependent CO/H2 shift. Transfer from EV053, EV065, GX007, and EV011 is methodological and donor-chemistry based, not direct proof for the exact P01 catalytic manifold.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This is a bounded characterization candidate rather than a catalytic proof card, and in that narrower role it is defensible. EV053 and EV065 support transferable fluorescence/UV-vis quenching logic in donor-containing cobalt photocatalytic contexts; GX007 directly supports that TEA substitution for TEOA can change upstream photochemical behavior; EV011 supports donor-specific radical/reactivity differences after quenching. The present design now explicitly uses a verified P01 baseline matrix, includes cobalt-containing and cobalt-free matched references, requires pilot checks for scattering and inner-filter distortion, and limits interpretation to descriptive donor-dependent upstream photoredox behavior rather than catalytic causation. Those restrictions directly address the main assignment concerns. It still cannot explain CO/H2 selectivity by itself, but as a descriptive covariate study tied to the verified matrix it is suitable for consideration.

E05 | Near-operando cobalt-state comparison under TEOA and TEA

Question: Assess whether TEA and TEOA correlate with different cobalt-associated oxidation or coordination signatures in the P01-like system under matched conditions, while keeping inference descriptive unless donor-linked differences survive donor-matched backgrounds and matched timing.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV059 anchors the closest supplied formulation family: CoCl2 plus bpy with g-C3N4 in MeCN/H2O using TEOA, with triethylamine noted among related donor variants. GX008 supports operando-capable spectroscopy and strict controls for photocatalytic CO2 reduction. EV046 supports catalyst-free/background references and warns that before/after or recovered-state characterization alone cannot identify the operating catalyst. Proposed transfer: compare TEA and TEOA only within a matched P01-like matrix, add donor-matched cobalt-free references for both donors, and interpret donor effects as correlation with cobalt-state signatures rather than proof of mechanism.

GX008, EV046, EV059

Supports: A reproducible TEA-versus-TEOA difference in cobalt-associated signatures under matched illuminated conditions, especially when smaller or absent before illumination and absent in donor-matched cobalt-free backgrounds, supports a donor-linked change in the cobalt-containing manifold.

Refutes: If TEA and TEOA show no reproducible difference in cobalt-associated signatures before or at the matched illuminated state within method resolution, a large donor-specific cobalt-state difference is disfavored as the main explanation for CO/H2 changes.

Inconclusive: Signals dominated by support or donor background, assignments visible equally in cobalt-free references, or differences seen only in recovered samples are inconclusive. Even a clear donor-dependent cobalt signature does not alone prove the active species or the cause of product selectivity.

Parameter selection: No exact spectroscopic settings are supplied. Use pilot scans to determine a measurable cobalt signal range, unsaturated acquisition settings, and the shortest illumination interval that produces a detectable state change without complete decomposition. Hold reactor geometry, gas handling, optical flux, and quench timing constant across donor conditions.

Limitations: Assignments in a mixed support-plus-molecular matrix remain uncertain, and before-versus-illuminated comparisons are time-coupled rather than isolated causal tests. This card addresses state correlation under matched donors, not full active-species identification or causal pathway separation.

Feasibility: specialist

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - As written, this is a descriptive near-operando characterization option with properly limited claims, not a mechanism proof. EV059 anchors the closest formulation family, while GX008 and EV046 support operando-capable spectroscopy, matched controls, and caution against overinterpreting before/after or recovered-state data. The revised card now includes donor-matched cobalt-free references for both donors at both sampling states, which materially improves signal assignment against donor/support backgrounds. It also states that conclusions are descriptive correlations unless donor-linked differences survive donor-matched backgrounds and matched timing, and it recommends one cobalt-sensitive readout plus an independent cross-check where feasible. Assignment uncertainty in a mixed matrix remains a practical limitation, but the card now treats that as a bounded characterization problem rather than as a basis for strong mechanistic claims. That makes it defensible for researcher planning despite specialist resources.

E06 | 13CO2 origin check for CO under matched TEOA and TEA conditions

Question: Determine whether CO detected in the matched donor comparison is CO2-derived under both donor identities, protecting the TEA/TEOA selectivity comparison against donor-specific carbon artifacts or background CO formation.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV021 reports that TEOA can participate non-innocently while isotope labeling still verified CO2 as the CO source. EV046, EV022, EV082, and GX008 support transferable use of 13CO2, GC-MS, and negative controls in photocatalytic CO2-reduction studies. Proposed transfer: apply donor-matched 13CO2 labeling and donor-matched catalyst-omission negatives to the same P01-like benchmark workflow, and interpret carbon origin separately from donor-effect or mechanism claims.

EV046, EV021, EV022, EV082, GX008

Supports: Detection of the expected labeled CO signature in both donor conditions, with corresponding unlabeled reference patterns in natural-abundance runs and little or no labeled CO in the donor-matched catalyst-omission controls, supports the claim that observed CO in the TEA/TEOA comparison is genuinely CO2-derived under both donors.

Refutes: If a donor condition shows CO without the expected labeled signature under 13CO2, or if comparable labeled CO appears in the matched catalyst-omission control for that donor, the CO comparison is undermined by artifact or background formation concerns.

Inconclusive: Very low CO signal, overlapping fragments, carryover, or inconsistent gas handling can leave origin assignment inconclusive. Even a positive 13CO2 result does not identify why TEA and TEOA differ; it only addresses carbon origin and catalyst dependence of detected CO.

Parameter selection: Use the same matched formulation, reactor, illumination program, and sampling schedule as the verified benchmark. Because no isotope-analysis recipe is supplied, first establish GC-MS discrimination between labeled and unlabeled CO with instrument-specific checks, then choose sampling volume and run time to detect the smallest benchmark CO signal without overload. If signal remains marginal, prioritize the donor-matched labeled pairs and donor-matched catalyst-omission negatives over expanding the matrix.

Limitations: This card verifies product origin and bounds catalyst dependence of labeled CO detection; it does not resolve whether donor effects arise from photophysics, proton management, medium composition, or cobalt-state changes. Before/after agreement in product origin cannot by itself establish the operating-state mechanism.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This is a strong supportive assay-validation candidate. EV021 directly supports the relevance of isotope checks because TEOA can be non-innocent while CO still derives from CO2. EV046, EV022, EV082, and GX008 support transferable use of 13CO2, GC-MS, and negative controls for product-origin verification. The revised card now includes donor-matched labeled and unlabeled catalyst-present runs plus donor-matched labeled no-cobalt negatives for both TEOA and TEA, which addresses the prior asymmetry concern. It also cleanly separates origin verification from mechanistic interpretation of donor effects and explicitly requires instrument-specific GC-MS discrimination checks and caution near detection limits. This does not explain why TEA and TEOA differ, but it directly protects the donor comparison from carbon-origin artifacts and is therefore valuable and inferentially sound for its stated purpose.

Complete samples, controls, contrasts and procedures

H02

Claim: TEA and TEOA alter upstream photoredox delivery by changing reductive-quenching efficiency or route, which then shifts electron flux into the cobalt-containing catalytic manifold. A discriminating prediction is that donor substitution changes quenching or transient reduced-state observables in the same direction as any catalytic selectivity change, whereas similar quenching behavior with divergent CO/H2 would argue for downstream causes.

Alternative: Quenching of the light-absorbing component is similar for TEA and TEOA, and any product shift arises mainly from later proton-transfer, substrate-binding, or catalyst-state effects rather than donor-side photoredox kinetics.

Platform and comparator choice: P01 is the main inference target, but P02 is the more discriminating supplementary platform for donor-side quenching because it already has cobalt/TEOA/Ru quenching evidence. The comparator is TEA-for-TEOA substitution with all non-donor components fixed within each platform.

Uncertainty: Most quenching evidence is transferable rather than direct for Co-bpy/g-C3N4, and donor-derived radicals observed elsewhere may not be the same dominant species in the target system. Support effects in P01 may blur direct mapping between photophysical observables and catalytic output.

E07 | Matched donor quenching in P01

Question: Test whether replacing TEOA with TEA changes absorber-side quenching or bleaching behavior in the primary Co-bpy/g-C3N4 platform while other formulation elements are held fixed.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV059 anchors a TEOA-based CoCl2/bpy/g-C3N4 CO2-reduction formulation as P01. EV053 and GX007 show fluorescence or UV-vis can discriminate donor-dependent quenching behavior and that TEA substitution can change photochemical fading rates in another cobalt-containing photochemical system. EV011 supports TEA and TEOA as reductive quenchers that form distinct donor-derived radicals. GX008 supports inclusion of omission controls and bounded interpretation. Proposed transfer: apply these observables within P01 only as characterization of upstream photophysics, not proof of the full catalytic mechanism.

EV053, EV011, GX007, EV059, GX008

Supports: Support for H02 would be a reproducible TEA-versus-TEOA difference in quenching or bleaching direction within cobalt-containing P01, with the donor contrast larger than instrumental/background variation and directionally consistent with any independently observed catalytic selectivity change. Similar donor differences in no-cobalt references would indicate an absorber-side donor effect; stronger differences only when cobalt is present would suggest donor-sensitive cobalt-associated pathways.

Refutes: Evidence against a mainly upstream donor-quenching explanation would be indistinguishable TEA and TEOA optical responses in the matched cobalt-containing P01 samples after background correction and calibration, especially if catalytic CO/H2 still differs under the same formulation. That outcome would shift weight toward downstream proton-transfer, substrate-binding, or catalyst-state explanations.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if scattering, inner-filter effects, donor absorbance, suspension instability, or uncontrolled pH/ionic-strength changes prevent reliable comparison; also inconclusive if donor differences appear only in multi-factor unmatched sample states or if optical changes cannot be separated from irreversible decomposition.

Parameter selection: Use the literature-defined P01 composition family as the chemical starting point but verify exact loadings and optical density from source or pilot rather than inventing values. Pilot by scanning sample dilution and path length until baseline absorbance and emission are within the linear detector range and g-C3N4 scattering does not saturate the signal. Use identical preparation age, mixing, and illumination dose across groups. If suspension measurements are unstable, pilot matched filtrate and suspension modes as separate sample states rather than mixing them in one contrast.

Limitations: Steady-state optical changes can indicate donor-dependent quenching or bleaching but cannot by themselves assign the reduced cobalt species, quantify electron flux into catalysis, or prove causation for CO/H2 selectivity. Support effects in P01 may make absorber and cobalt contributions overlap. No-cobalt references do not recreate the full catalytic manifold. If multiple factors such as donor identity and pH drift co-vary, causal attribution remains limited.

Feasibility: low_cost

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This candidate remains a well-bounded characterization experiment in the main P01 formulation family. EV059 directly anchors the target formulation family, while EV053, GX007, and EV011 support donor-sensitive optical observables and the plausibility that TEA versus TEOA can alter upstream quenching-related behavior. The comparison structure is disciplined: the primary contrast changes donor identity only within P01, and the no-donor plus no-cobalt references appropriately bound background and cobalt-associated contributions. The card also keeps inference limited to upstream photophysical characterization rather than claiming catalytic causation or specific intermediate identity. Known confounders such as scattering, inner-filter effects, decomposition, filtrate-versus-suspension ambiguity, and donor-driven medium changes are explicitly acknowledged as limits rather than ignored. For the stated descriptive question, no substantive unresolved flaw requires downgrading.

E08 | Transient reduced-state comparison

Question: Determine whether TEA and TEOA produce different transient amplitudes or decay kinetics in the P01 cobalt-containing formulation, while bounding interpretation if donor substitution also changes bulk medium or interfacial properties.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV007 and EV041 support that TEOA can participate in reductive quenching and alter reduced-intermediate lifetimes in photocatalytic systems. EV018 and EV005 support transient methods for donor-sensitive carrier kinetics but also show platform dependence and incomplete species assignment. GX008 supports negative controls and TAS-type characterization in photocatalytic systems. Proposed transfer: compare donor-dependent transient observables in P01 as descriptive kinetic signatures, using no-donor and no-cobalt references and a medium-adjustment pilot to narrow overassignment rather than claiming a specific cobalt intermediate.

EV007, EV018, EV005, EV041, GX008

Supports: Support for H02 would be a reproducible TEA versus TEOA difference in transient formation, amplitude, or persistence in the full P01 formulation, with donor-enabled signals above no-donor background and a contrast that is absent or smaller in no-cobalt references or that persists after medium-adjustment pilots. This supports donor-associated kinetic differences consistent with altered upstream photoredox delivery, while remaining agnostic about exact intermediate identity unless cross-checked independently.

Refutes: Evidence against a strong upstream donor-kinetics account would be closely matched TEA and TEOA transient observables in cobalt-containing P01 under both baseline and medium-adjusted conditions, provided the method is sensitive enough to detect donor-enabled signals and controls behave as expected. If catalytic selectivity still differs elsewhere under such matched transient behavior, interpretation shifts toward downstream chemistry or unobserved states.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if signals are too weak, unstable, or dominated by scattering, photothermal artifacts, or degradation; if donor substitution changes bulk-medium properties so strongly that medium-adjusted comparison is not feasible; if no-cobalt references show similar donor differences that cannot be separated from cobalt effects; or if assignments remain too uncertain to connect the transient contrast to the catalytic manifold beyond a descriptive observation.

Parameter selection: Select probe wavelengths and fitting windows by pilot spectra on donor-containing and no-donor samples, choosing regions with adequate signal-to-noise and minimal artifact. Set pump fluence below nonlinear or damaging regimes by fluence-dependence checks. Keep preparation age, optical density range, and path length matched across groups. If medium adjustment is attempted, choose adjustments only after measuring which bulk properties differ most between TEA and TEOA in the actual samples; do not assume a literature recipe. If adjustment introduces new instability, report only the baseline descriptive comparison.

Limitations: Species assignment in a heterogeneous supported system is provisional, and transient differences do not uniquely prove electron flow into a specific cobalt intermediate. Donor substitution can co-vary pH, viscosity, ionic environment, adsorption, and suspension stability, so even a clear transient difference may reflect combined effects. No-cobalt controls help bound interpretation but do not recreate the same interfacial environment as cobalt-containing samples.

Feasibility: specialist

Review status: revise

API review: revise - This is still a valuable candidate, but its interpretive scope must remain narrow. The evidence base is transferable rather than direct: EV007 and EV041 support donor-enabled reduced-state formation or lifetime changes in other photocatalytic systems, while EV018 and EV005 support transient methods for donor-sensitive carrier dynamics but also emphasize platform dependence and incomplete assignment. Compared with the prior review, the design is improved by explicit no-donor and no-cobalt references, measurement of bulk covariates, and a medium-adjustment pilot. Those additions materially strengthen the experiment as a descriptive kinetic comparison. However, they do not fully resolve the core inferential limits in supported P01: species assignment remains insecure, and medium adjustment cannot guarantee isolation of donor-side photoredox effects from coupled bulk/interfacial changes. The candidate should therefore be retained only with revised claims that any donor difference is descriptive evidence for donor-associated kinetic differences under these conditions, not a secure readout of electron flux into a defined cobalt state.

  • Transient signal assignment in heterogeneous P01 remains provisional, so TEA/TEOA-dependent traces cannot be confidently attributed to a specific cobalt-manifold intermediate without independent corroboration.
  • Donor substitution can still co-vary bulk and interfacial properties such as pH/basicity, scattering, adsorption, and suspension behavior; even with medium-adjustment pilots, a transient difference may reflect mixed medium effects rather than cleaner upstream electron-delivery change.

E09 | Supplementary P02 quenching transfer

Question: Use the more chemically defined homogeneous cobalt/Ru platform P02 as a supplementary test of whether TEA versus TEOA changes donor-side quenching behavior when support effects are minimized.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV065 provides a cobalt photocatalytic CO2-reduction platform with Ru photosensitizer and TEOA plus quenching evidence, making P02 suitable for a supplementary donor-substitution test. EV053 and EV077 support luminescence or UV-vis quenching as mechanistic probes in donor-containing cobalt-related photochemical systems. EV011 supports TEA and TEOA as reductive quenchers that form donor-derived radicals. Proposed transfer: hold the P02 catalyst and photosensitizer constant, substitute TEA for TEOA, and compare quenching directionality without claiming that absolute kinetics or mechanism are identical to P01.

EV065, EV053, EV077, EV011

Supports: Support for H02 would be a reproducible TEA-versus-TEOA quenching difference in P02, particularly if the effect direction agrees with P01 characterization and any P01 catalytic selectivity trend. Agreement across platforms would strengthen the idea that donor identity can alter upstream photoredox delivery rather than acting only through P01-specific support effects.

Refutes: If TEA and TEOA behave indistinguishably in P02 despite clear method sensitivity and P01 still shows large CO/H2 differences, that weakens a general donor-side quenching explanation and points toward platform-specific downstream causes. It would not alone falsify a P01-specific upstream effect because the platforms differ.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if donor substitution changes solubility, excited-state background, or acid-base conditions enough to obscure clean Stern-Volmer behavior; also inconclusive if P02 quenching differs but P01 does not, because that could reflect real platform dependence rather than a universal donor effect.

Parameter selection: Use the P02 formulation family exactly as anchored by EV065 for component identity, but verify concentrations and optical range from the source or pilot. Select donor and photosensitizer ranges where the baseline emission is measurable and quenching analysis remains within the detector's linear regime. If Stern-Volmer plots curve or deviate strongly, report relative quenching descriptively rather than forcing a single rate interpretation. Keep solvent family, deoxygenation, CO2 saturation, and illumination history matched across groups.

Limitations: This is a supplementary system, not the main inference target. Even a clean donor effect in P02 cannot prove that P01 shares the same mechanistic route because catalyst architecture, absorber identity, and support effects differ. Conversely, lack of a P02 effect does not eliminate a P01-specific donor influence. The experiment is best used for directional transfer, not quantitative mechanistic equivalence.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This remains a sound supplementary candidate. EV065 anchors a homogeneous cobalt/Ru/TEOA platform with quenching observability, and EV053, EV077, and EV011 support using luminescence or UV-vis quenching to probe donor-side behavior and justify TEA-for-TEOA substitution as a meaningful comparison. The design is appropriately bounded: it does not claim that P02 is mechanistically equivalent to P01, but instead uses P02 as a cleaner supplementary system for directional transfer of donor-side effects when support complications are minimized. The no-donor and no-cobalt references strengthen interpretation by separating donor-to-photosensitizer effects from cobalt-involved contributions. Because the stated role is supplementary and transfer limits are preserved, there is no major unresolved flaw for the stated question.
