E10 | Matched TEA/TEOA photophysics-to-products correlation in the Co-bpy baseline, with optional homogeneous bridge

Question: Test whether TEA-versus-TEOA substitution changes donor-side quenching or transient reduced-state observables in the same direction as any CO/H2 shift under otherwise matched conditions, while limiting inference to correlation-versus-discordance rather than causation.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV053 and EV065 support donor-sensitive quenching measurements in cobalt-containing photocatalytic systems, EV007 supports tracking reduced intermediates and CO2-responsive transient behavior in molecular photocatalysis, GX007 shows TEA-for-TEOA substitution can alter irradiation-time photochemistry, and EV081 supports quantifying both CO and H2 with catalyst-omission backgrounds. The proposal transfers these observations into a matched TEA-versus-TEOA comparison in the recommended Co-bpy platform. Any concordance between photophysical and catalytic shifts would remain supportive correlation, not proof that donor-side photophysics is the sole cause of selectivity.

EV053, EV065, EV007, EV081, GX007

Supports: H02 is supported if, across matched TEA and TEOA runs in P01, donor substitution reproducibly shifts at least one donor-side quenching or transient observable and catalytic CO/H2 output changes in the same directional sense, while cobalt-omission controls do not account for most of the product shift. Similar qualitative directionality in optional P02 would strengthen plausibility that donor-side behavior contributes, but would still not establish causation or a shared mechanism.

Refutes: H02 is weakened if TEA and TEOA give similar donor-side quenching and transient observables within experimental resolution in P01, yet CO/H2 differs reproducibly beyond the bounded background seen in cobalt-omission controls. That outcome would favor downstream explanations such as proton management, catalyst-state, substrate-binding, or other donor-induced changes not captured by the selected photophysical observables.

Inconclusive: The result is inconclusive if TEA and TEOA formulations cannot be matched reproducibly, if photophysical signals are below assignment confidence or dominated by scattering or support artifacts, if sample-state linkage between photophysics and catalysis is not demonstrated, if background product formation is too large to isolate cobalt-dependent behavior, or if multiple observables shift in inconsistent directions.

Parameter selection: Do not invent donor concentration, catalyst loading, light intensity, or sampling times. Start from the literature-anchored P01 baseline formulation and verify exact values from the supplied system recommendation. Before the full comparison, run a pilot to choose a dilution window that keeps optical signals measurable without changing component identities, confirms linear detector response, and avoids complete inner-filter saturation. Choose catalytic sampling times by bracketing early, mid, and later illumination intervals where both CO and H2 are above detection and cobalt-omission controls remain interpretable. For each measurement block, first confirm that the defined pre-measurement history yields stable backgrounds and comparable spectra in duplicate preparations. For the optional P02 bridge, first confirm observable quenching in the TEOA formulation before introducing TEA as the single-variable comparator.

Limitations: This design tests correlation versus discordance, not causation. Even reproducible concordance between donor-side observables and CO/H2 does not exclude coupled changes in proton transfer, catalyst speciation, substrate binding, or other downstream chemistry. P01 support effects may decouple measured optical observables from the chemically active manifold. Cobalt-omission contrasts are necessity/background tests because omitting cobalt changes the reaction manifold. The optional P02 bridge improves photophysical interpretability but is not chemically identical to the Co-bpy/g-C3N4 target, so only bounded directionality transfer is justified.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: revise

API review: revise - This is a high-value integrative candidate, but it still needs tighter framing and implementation detail. The evidence supports the concept: EV053, EV007, EV065, and GX007 justify donor-sensitive photophysical characterization, and EV081 supports measuring both CO and H2 with catalyst-omission controls. The current card improves on the previous version by explicitly stating that the goal is correlation versus discordance, not proof of causation, and by limiting the optional P02 bridge to directionality transfer. Those are real improvements. Still, the central limitation remains inherent: concordance between photophysical and catalytic shifts does not isolate donor-side photoredox delivery as the driver. In addition, because the design links multiple readout types across possibly different measurement blocks, interpretation depends on stronger researcher-level planning for matched sample state and pre-measurement history than is currently specified. The experiment should therefore be retained in revised form, with conclusions explicitly bounded to concordance testing and with clearer planning for how sample comparability will be demonstrated.

  • Matched movement of donor-side photophysical observables and CO/H2 outputs would provide correlation only; the design cannot by itself distinguish upstream donor-side causation from parallel donor-induced changes in proton management, catalyst speciation, substrate binding, or other downstream chemistry.
  • The integrated design still leaves sample-state linkage across photophysics and catalysis underdefined; without clearly matched composition, aging, illumination history, and justified sub-mixture equivalence, the intended photophysics-to-products comparison can be difficult to interpret.
  • The optional P02 bridge remains supportive only for bounded directionality transfer; agreement or disagreement across platforms stays mechanistically ambiguous and must not be overread.
Complete samples, controls, contrasts and procedures

E11 | Donor-radical detection under TEA versus TEOA substitution

Question: Test whether replacing TEOA with TEA changes detectable donor-derived radical signatures under illumination in a matched cobalt comparison matrix. This is a bounded donor-side characterization experiment, not a direct product-causation test.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV016 reports EPR spin-trapping detection of TEOA-derived radicals and notes similar TEA-derived adducts with TEOA line broadening attributed to viscosity; EV011 supports that both TEA and TEOA can generate alpha-aminoalkyl radical reductants after excited-state quenching; GX018 provides the transferable framework that sacrificial-donor radical cation and radical species can arise in photochemical quenching while their mechanistic role remains uncertain. Proposed transfer: compare TEA and TEOA within one matched cobalt-containing formulation, include donor-matched dark and no-absorber controls, and limit inference to donor-side radical observables.

EV016, EV011, GX018

Supports: Support for H02 would be a reproducible donor-substitution effect under matched conditions in which TEA and TEOA differ in radical-associated signal behavior, both illuminated donor-containing samples exceed their donor-matched dark controls, and donor-matched no-absorber controls remain clearly smaller.

Refutes: Evidence against H02 as a dominant explanation would be closely similar TEA and TEOA radical-detection behavior across repeated matched runs with clean light dependence and low donor-matched background, especially if linked quenching observables are also similar while catalysis still diverges.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive outcomes include no resolved signal for either donor, strong probe or background chemistry, severe overlap or broadening preventing assignment, absorber instability during acquisition, or donor differences comparable to donor-matched no-absorber backgrounds. A radical-associated signal alone does not establish productive catalysis or control of CO versus H2.

Parameter selection: Do not fix donor loading, probe concentration, or irradiation time from unrelated systems. Use pilot series to choose the lowest probe level giving a stable baseline and the shortest acquisition window that resolves a light-dependent signal without obvious bleaching or runaway background. After calibration, keep solvent matrix, atmosphere, irradiation geometry, and acquisition settings identical across TEA and TEOA.

Limitations: Radical trapping can perturb chemistry, and detected adducts may be secondary trapped products rather than the immediate quenching intermediate. TEA and TEOA can differ in viscosity and line broadening, so spectral differences are not automatically identity differences. If only a supplementary readable platform works, transfer back to the main catalytic platform remains limited. This experiment does not measure CO or H2.

Feasibility: specialist

Review status: revise

API review: revise - This specialist candidate remains plausible and potentially useful, but only as a bounded donor-side characterization study. EV016 and EV011 support that TEA and TEOA can generate detectable donor-derived radical species and that EPR spin trapping can reveal donor-dependent observables, while GX018 appropriately cautions that the mechanistic role of these radicals remains uncertain. Relative to the prior review, the control structure is improved: donor-matched dark controls and donor-matched no-absorber controls are now explicit, and the card also warns that linewidth differences may arise from viscosity rather than radical identity. That materially fixes a prior confounding problem. The remaining concerns are interpretive rather than purely structural: even a clean donor-dependent radical signal difference cannot be taken as evidence for the productive catalytic pathway, and if readability requires shifting to a different platform, relevance to the main target is only transferable with limits. The candidate is therefore worth retaining conditionally, but only with conservative claims and pilot-dependent platform choice.

  • Radical-trap signals may reflect secondary trapped products, probe-induced perturbation, or donor-dependent linewidth and viscosity effects rather than differences in the immediate productive donor-radical route.
  • If the only spectroscopically readable implementation is moved to a supplementary platform rather than the main target context, transfer back to P01 remains limited.

H03

Claim: TEA/TEOA differences arise mainly because the amine changes proton-management and local CO2 environment, not only electron donation; TEOA may favor different proton or CO2-associated states than TEA. A discriminating prediction is that donor-dependent CO/H2 differences track medium or isotope-sensitive observables and can weaken when proton-delivery or solvent/composition variables are tightly normalized.

Alternative: The dominant TEA/TEOA effect is intrinsic donor redox chemistry or cobalt-state formation, and matching proton-source or medium variables will not substantially collapse the selectivity difference.

Platform and comparator choice: P01 is preferred because the donor substitution can be anchored to one Co-bpy baseline while keeping MeCN/H2O and g-C3N4 constant; TEA and TEOA are treated as multifunctional additives rather than pure reductants. No extra platform is needed unless P01 cannot separate donor identity from medium shifts.

Uncertainty: Evidence for CO2 capture, proton relay, or adduct formation is cross-system and often interpretive, so transfer to Co-bpy remains speculative until matched controls are done. TEA and TEOA likely change several coupled properties at once, making full causal separation difficult.

E13 | Medium-normalized TEA vs TEOA with attribution controls

Question: Test whether the TEA/TEOA CO/H2 difference in the P01 Co-bpy/g-C3N4 system persists after tighter recipe matching, while embedding internal attribution controls so any collapse of the difference is interpreted only as reduced compatibility with a strong medium-dominant explanation rather than proof of a specific proton-management mechanism.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV059 directly anchors the CoCl2+bpy/g-C3N4/MeCN:H2O/TEOA platform and notes TEA as a related donor option. EV061, EV013, GX009, and EV031 are transferred only as source observations that amines and solvent environments can alter CO2 association, proton availability, viscosity, conductivity, coordination environment, and intermediate stability. The proposal therefore compares TEA and TEOA within one anchored formulation family, adds no-amine and Ar controls for attribution, and narrows inference because composition normalization cannot make TEA and TEOA chemically equivalent.

EV061, EV013, GX009, EV059, EV031

Supports: Supports the medium-dominant version of H03 only in a narrowed sense if a TEA/TEOA difference evident in the anchored recipe becomes substantially smaller or disappears in the normalized comparison, while the CO2 and no-amine controls show that the normalized experiment still produces attributable signal. This would indicate that recipe-linked medium effects contribute materially, without identifying which hidden variable is responsible.

Refutes: Refutes the stronger medium-dominant emphasis of H03 if the TEA/TEOA difference is reproduced with similar direction and magnitude in both anchored and normalized comparisons, suggesting that the controlled composition variables are not the main source of the selectivity difference. That outcome would be more compatible with donor-specific redox, coordination, or cobalt-state alternatives, though not uniquely proving any one of them.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if one or more normalized groups give signal near detection limits, if normalization causes incompatible catalyst stability or phase behavior, if the Ar or no-amine controls reveal strong background artifacts, or if the observed TEA/TEOA differences are comparable to run-to-run analytical uncertainty.

Parameter selection: Do not invent literature values. Use the EV059-linked recipe family only to choose the starting composition logic, then pilot the minimum necessary executable settings: catalyst loading window, support amount, irradiation time, sampling interval, and gas calibration range. For normalized groups, hold MeCN/H2O ratio and total amine-derived fraction constant across TEA and TEOA where physically feasible; if miscibility or handling prevents exact matching, document the deviation and narrow the inference rather than asserting equivalence.

Limitations: TEA and TEOA remain chemically nonequivalent even after composition normalization, so this card cannot isolate proton activity, dissolved CO2, viscosity, conductivity, and cobalt coordination effects from one another. The no-amine comparison also changes overall liquid composition and therefore tests functional necessity more than pure mechanistic causality. Product data alone cannot identify the active cobalt species or the dominant hidden variable behind any selectivity shift.

Feasibility: low_cost

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This candidate remains defensible for researcher consideration because its stated question is appropriately bounded: whether a TEA/TEOA CO/H2 difference in the P01 family survives tighter composition normalization with attribution controls. The design is anchored by direct system evidence for the CoCl2+bpy/g-C3N4/MeCN:H2O/TEOA family, includes TEA as a related donor option, and now contains internal Ar and no-amine controls that bound carbon-product attribution and additive necessity. Importantly, the card does not claim that equalized recipe logic makes TEA and TEOA chemically equivalent, and it explicitly limits interpretation of any collapse in donor difference to reduced compatibility with a strong medium-dominant account rather than proof of a specific proton-management mechanism. Residual multi-property confounding remains, but it is acknowledged and kept within the inference limits rather than hidden.

Researcher decision: [ ] Select [ ] Modify before selection [ ] Defer

E14 | Isotope-sensitive operando signatures in TEA vs TEOA Co-bpy photocatalysis

Question: Test whether TEA and TEOA produce different H/D-sensitive spectroscopic observables under one matched P01 configuration, consistent with different proton-coupled environments or hydrogen-containing states, while avoiding overclaim that isotope effects identify a unique intermediate.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV080 supports isotope substitution as a transferable assignment aid in operando FTIR-type measurements, not species identity in this Co-bpy system. GX010 and GX015 support proton-inventory and isotope logic as transferable discriminators of proton-delivery environments in cobalt CO2-reduction chemistry, but not direct proof for this photocatalyst. EV031 supports the transferable idea that donor or solvent environment can change detectable intermediate populations and selectivity in bipyridine-based CO2 reduction. Proposed transfer: use one preselected operando or near-operando spectroscopic mode for matched TEA and TEOA samples, compare H2O-containing and D2O-containing media within each donor, and interpret any isotope-sensitive donor difference only as evidence for differing hydrogen-coupled observables under these conditions.

EV080, GX010, GX015, EV031

Supports: Support for H03 would be a reproducible donor-dependent isotope response under matched acquisition: TEA and TEOA differ in H/D-sensitive band positions, magnitudes, or time behavior beyond donor-free or baseline changes. Stronger but still non-decisive support occurs if donor-dependent CO and H2 divergence also changes in pattern under the same isotopic substitution.

Refutes: Evidence against H03 would be that TEA and TEOA show no reproducible difference in isotope-sensitive observables under matched conditions, while spectra are dominated by shared or non-isotope-sensitive changes. If catalytic TEA and TEOA differences remain substantial in matched runs despite indistinguishable H/D-sensitive signatures, a proton-management-centered explanation is weakened relative to alternatives.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if suspension scattering or baseline drift obscures donor comparisons, if overlapping support or solvent features dominate assignment, if D2O substitution changes bulk medium properties enough that spectral differences cannot be bounded to hydrogen-coupled observables, or if donor-dependent changes are present but not isotope-sensitive.

Parameter selection: Use the literature-anchored P01 formulation family only after verifying the exact baseline composition from supplied source records. Calibrate path length, catalyst loading, aqueous fraction, and acquisition interval by pilot criteria of stable baseline, detectable catalyst-region signal, and repeatable spectra across replicate preparations. Predefine acceptable baseline-drift and scattering thresholds before comparing donors. Keep preparation order and nominal component identities matched between H2O-containing and D2O-containing groups. If pH or pD matching is attempted, document one convention and use it consistently without claiming exact equivalence.

Limitations: This card can test whether TEA and TEOA differ in isotope-sensitive observables under matched conditions, but it cannot uniquely identify intermediates, prove rate-determining roles, or isolate proton management from all donor-dependent medium changes. D2O substitution is itself a multi-property perturbation, and near-operando measurements may miss the true operating state.

Feasibility: specialist

Review status: revise

API review: revise - This is still a potentially valuable specialist characterization screen, but not yet a shortlist item. The card improved by fixing the logic to one predefined operando or near-operando mode per execution, by adding donor-free reference, and by narrowing interpretation to hydrogen-coupled observables rather than unique intermediate identities. Those changes materially reduce prior overreach. However, the core inferential problem remains: in this heterogeneous suspension, reproducible isotope-sensitive spectral differences would still be difficult to assign confidently, and D2O changes more than proton transfer. The candidate therefore has research value as a pilot-led mechanistic screen, but its conclusions remain assignment-limited and need careful revision before stronger researcher planning.

  • Signal assignment remains substantially ambiguous in a mixed Co-bpy/g-C3N4 suspension, so donor-dependent isotope-sensitive features may still reflect support, solvent, scattering, or cobalt-speciation changes rather than the intended hydrogen-coupled states.
  • D2O substitution remains a multi-property perturbation, so donor-dependent isotope responses cannot cleanly separate proton-management effects from other solvent-isotope consequences.
  • The exact spectroscopic implementation is still pilot-defined rather than fully specified, so cross-group comparability depends on successful preliminary mode selection and quality thresholds.

    "Parallel CO and H2 product amounts from matched runs to test co-occurrence with selectivity differences without equating correlation to cause"

E15 | CO2-associated donor speciation under matched P01 medium

Question: Test whether TEA and TEOA create different detectable CO2-associated solution states in the P01 medium before illumination or after reaction-relevant equilibration, as a bounded indicator of donor-dependent local CO2/proton environment.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV029 and EV061 motivate checking whether TEOA can participate in CO2-associated adduct or ion-pair chemistry beyond electron donation. GX009 adds transferable concern that cobalt-bipyridine media can change with solvent coordination, water, viscosity, and conductivity. EV031 supports the broader idea that donor/solvent environment can alter observable intermediates and selectivity. Proposed transfer: in matched P01 solvent composition, compare TEA and TEOA by descriptive solution-state spectroscopy. This proposal does not assume the same adduct identity reported in other systems, and any observed bands or shifts would indicate association only, not the catalytic active state or causal mechanism by themselves.

EV029, EV061, GX009, EV031

Supports: Support for H03 would be a reproducible donor-dependent difference in CO2-responsive spectral features under matched P01 medium, especially if TEOA and TEA show distinct changes relative to their own inert references and the no-amine background. This would support a local CO2/proton-environment contribution, while remaining associational until linked to catalytic outcomes under the same controls.

Refutes: Evidence against H03-dominant interpretation would be no reproducible donor-specific CO2-responsive difference despite adequate detection sensitivity and clean backgrounds, especially if TEA and TEOA appear spectroscopically similar under matched medium. That would weaken the case that donor-linked CO2-associated speciation is a main explanation, though it would not prove a redox-only mechanism.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if spectra are dominated by solvent/support overlap, if signals are near detection limits, if assignments depend on a single ambiguous band, or if donor substitution also changes uncontrolled covariates enough that the contrast becomes multi-factor in practice. Observing a new signal without assignment controls cannot establish adduct identity or catalytic relevance.

Parameter selection: Do not invent fixed acquisition settings. Pilot first to choose sample concentration, path length, equilibration time, and whether IR, NMR, or both have usable signal-to-background in the actual P01 medium. Set the lowest concentration that still gives reproducible donor and CO2-responsive features without obvious precipitation or severe scattering from g-C3N4. Use the same preparation order, gas-contact protocol, and delay before measurement across groups. If g-C3N4 prevents interpretable solution-state spectra, run a matched clarified-supernatant variant as a bounded adaptation and label the transfer limitation explicitly rather than claiming fully equivalent operating chemistry.

Limitations: This characterization is descriptive. A donor-specific CO2-responsive signal cannot by itself prove the catalytic intermediate, the active cobalt state, or the cause of CO/H2 selectivity. g-C3N4 and cobalt-bpy may contribute overlapping features, and dark-equilibrated samples may miss illumination-generated states. If donor identity changes pH, coordination, or suspension properties simultaneously, the comparison remains mechanistically mixed even when composition is nominally matched.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This remains a strong bounded characterization candidate. The question is descriptive and well matched to the controls: whether TEA and TEOA produce different detectable CO2-responsive solution-state signatures in matched P01 medium before illumination or after dark equilibration. The design includes within-donor inert versus CO2 contrasts, a no-amine background, and allowance for component-minus-one references. It also explicitly states that any donor-specific signal would indicate association only, not catalytic active-state identity or causal mechanism. The clarified-supernatant fallback is transparently labeled as a transfer limitation rather than treated as equivalent chemistry. Because the card asks only what the proposed measurements can support, no major issue blocks shortlist.

E16 | Amine omission and bounded inert-base replacement

Question: Test whether amine presence is necessary in the P01 TEA/TEOA comparison and whether a tightly screened non-donor base can produce only limited medium-related shifts, without treating it as an amine surrogate or proof of proton-management causality.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV013 supports treating TEA and TEOA as multifunctional additives that can act as H+ source and hole scavenger, so omission is required before interpreting additive identity effects. EV024, GX010, and GX015 support the narrower transferable idea that proton-delivery environment can alter CO2-reduction behavior, which justifies a bounded non-donor-base probe. GX008 supports matched negative controls and broad product analysis. Transfer remains limited because no supplied evidence shows that a non-donor base reproduces the sacrificial-donor role in the target Co-bpy photocatalysis; therefore any shift with base addition is only descriptive evidence for medium contribution.

EV013, EV024, GX010, GX015, GX008

Supports: H03 is supported only in a limited sense if amine omission strongly suppresses or shifts the baseline behavior and the pre-screened non-donor base causes a reproducible partial movement in activity or CO/H2 distribution relative to no amine, while TEA and TEOA still differ under matched conditions.

Refutes: H03 is weakened if the non-donor base produces no reproducible shift relative to no amine and TEA versus TEOA differences remain large under otherwise matched conditions, especially if omission mainly removes activity in a manner consistent with loss of sacrificial-donor function alone.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if the selected base introduces background reactivity, coordination changes, instability, or phase changes; if bulk acid-base comparison is not meaningful in the medium; or if CO and H2 signals are near detection limits.

Parameter selection: Do not invent concentrations. Use the verified P01 baseline for TEA and TEOA conditions. For the replacement arm, screen candidate non-donor bases in the same solvent matrix and select one only if it satisfies all pilot criteria: negligible CO and H2 in catalyst-free or no-light blanks, no obvious decomposition or precipitation before irradiation, and a measurable change in a bulk acid-base descriptor relative to E16G02. If no candidate satisfies these criteria, retain only E16G01, E16G02, and E16G04.

Limitations: The replacement arm remains intrinsically limited because a non-donor base cannot reproduce sacrificial electron donation. Any observed rescue can also arise from coordination, ionic-strength, viscosity, or solvation changes. Failure to rescue does not exclude proton-management contributions. Bulk acid-base descriptors in MeCN/H2O may not represent local proton delivery. This card therefore tests necessity plus bounded medium sensitivity, not full causal separation.

Feasibility: low_cost

Review status: revise

API review: revise - This candidate is improved and worth retaining conditionally, but revise remains the correct decision. The omission arm is useful because it tests whether an amine is functionally necessary under the P01 baseline, and the direct TEA/TEOA arm remains a valid multifunctional-additive comparison. The revision also strengthened the replacement arm by requiring pilot screening for low blank reactivity, mixture compatibility, and measurable bulk acid-base shift, and by permitting that arm to be dropped if no suitable base exists. Even so, the core inferential limitation remains substantial: a non-donor base is not an amine surrogate in this photocatalytic context, and any movement in activity or selectivity can arise from several medium changes unrelated to the target interpretation. The candidate is therefore useful as a bounded intervention screen, but still needs cautious framing rather than shortlist status.

  • The bounded replacement arm still depends on an unspecified pre-screened non-donor base whose coordination, ionic-strength, solvation, or side-reactivity effects could confound interpretation beyond proton-management changes.
  • The rescue logic remains intrinsically non-exclusive: partial rescue by the replacement base would be only descriptive medium evidence, while failure to rescue would not exclude proton-management contributions because sacrificial donation is not reproduced.
  • The E16G03 versus E16G04 comparison remains explicitly confounded by simultaneous change in amine presence and base presence, so it cannot support mechanistic ranking beyond a weak descriptive bound.

E17 | TEA/TEOA water-fraction crossover map in the P01 baseline

Question: Test whether the TEA-versus-TEOA CO/H2 difference depends strongly on MeCN/H2O composition, consistent with donor effects arising largely from proton management and local CO2/medium properties rather than donor identity alone.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: Source observations are cross-system: TEOA proportion and water/solvent environment can alter CO2 reduction output and intermediate visibility, and TEA/TEOA can function as proton-source/hole-scavenging co-solvents. Proposed transfer is a controlled within-P01 donor comparison where only donor identity and water fraction are varied in a balanced map. This proposal does not assume the same intermediates or mechanisms are present in Co-bpy/g-C3N4; it tests whether the donor effect collapses or crosses over under tighter medium control.

GX009, EV061, EV013, EV031

Supports: Support for H03 would be a clear donor-by-water interaction: TEA and TEOA differ strongly at one MeCN/H2O setting but the difference narrows, disappears, or crosses over at the other, with accompanying shifts in medium descriptors. That pattern would indicate that the donor effect is substantially mediated by proton-management or local medium/CO2 environment rather than being fixed by donor identity alone.

Refutes: Evidence against H03 would be that TEA and TEOA show a similar directional CO/H2 difference of comparable magnitude at both water settings while each donor is otherwise stable and analytically well matched. That would favor an explanation dominated by donor-specific redox or cobalt-state effects that are not substantially collapsed by this medium normalization.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive outcomes include very low product formation, unstable phase behavior, unequal catalyst suspension or light absorption across water settings, analytical uncertainty larger than the donor effect, or simultaneous large changes in conductivity/dispersion that prevent attributing differences specifically to proton-management versus other medium changes.

Parameter selection: Do not invent solvent fractions. Choose two water settings by pilot within the literature-supported MeCN/H2O family: one near the current baseline and one more water-rich, but only if both remain operationally comparable for TEA and TEOA. Hold total solvent volume constant. Keep the same nominal donor condition for TEA and TEOA as an initial comparator, then verify by pilot that neither donor causes immediate deactivation, phase separation, or complete quenching failure. If apparent pH is not reliable in the mixed solvent, record it only as a bounded descriptor and prioritize conductivity and product outputs.

Limitations: This card cannot isolate a unique cause because changing water fraction also changes viscosity, conductivity, CO2 solubility, donor protonation, and catalyst/support environment. Even single-factor pairwise contrasts are embedded in a system where effective photon absorption and cobalt speciation may shift with medium. A crossover is therefore strong descriptive evidence for medium involvement but not proof of a specific proton-relay or CO2-adduct mechanism. No claim of matched chemistry should be made from generic donor labels alone.

Feasibility: low_cost

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This remains a well-bounded descriptive interaction map suited to the stated hypothesis. It asks whether the TEA/TEOA CO/H2 difference depends strongly on water fraction within the P01 family, using balanced donor comparisons at two pilot-selected MeCN/H2O settings that must remain operationally comparable. The design explicitly treats any donor-by-water interaction, collapse, or crossover as evidence for medium involvement rather than proof of a unique proton-relay or CO2-adduct mechanism. It also builds in practical comparability checks for phase behavior, common analytics, and matched handling. Because the question is descriptive and the inference is limited to donor-by-medium dependence, the candidate remains suitable for researcher consideration without major revision.

E18 | Matched donor comparison with stable bulk descriptors

Question: Test whether TEA versus TEOA differences in the P01 Co-bpy/g-C3N4 system reproducibly co-vary with directly measured bulk proton-management and medium descriptors, while keeping the conclusion descriptive rather than causal.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: The sources support treating TEA and TEOA as multifunctional additives that can alter proton supply, CO2-associated solution behavior, conductivity, viscosity, and related medium properties, and they show that isotope-sensitive proton-transfer tests can be mechanistically informative in other cobalt systems. The proposal transfers only the need to measure donor-dependent covariates alongside catalytic outputs within one fixed baseline formulation. It does not assume that bulk descriptors equal the local environment at cobalt or g-C3N4, and it excludes the previously overreaching CO2-uptake proxy as a core readout.

GX009, EV061, GX010, EV013

Supports: Support for H03 would be a reproducible association in which TEA and TEOA show different CO/H2 behavior together with different stable bulk descriptor values or shifts, and the matched D2O perturbation alters the donor contrast in a way consistent across repeats. This supports a contribution from medium or proton-management differences, not a complete mechanism.

Refutes: Evidence against H03 as the main explanation would be that TEA and TEOA give reproducibly different CO/H2 outputs while the validated bulk descriptors remain indistinguishable within uncertainty and the isotope perturbation does not materially change the donor contrast. That would shift attention toward explanations such as donor-dependent quenching or cobalt-state formation.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if catalytic differences are not reproducible, if acidity or conductivity measurements are unstable in the mixed suspension, if isotope substitution causes uncontrolled formulation changes, or if descriptor differences appear without any consistent relation to product behavior across matched repeats. Bulk correlation cannot establish local interfacial conditions or specific intermediates.

Parameter selection: Use the literature-anchored P01 formulation only as the starting recipe and verify any missing numerical settings from the supplied source set before execution. First run a pilot in the actual reaction medium to choose one acidity-proxy method that is stable, calibrated, and minimally perturbing; use that single method for every group rather than mixing pH and pD scales. In the same pilot, confirm that conductivity readings are repeatable in catalyst-containing suspensions and define a fixed sampling delay after CO2 equilibration and after irradiation. If D2O materially changes miscibility, suspension stability, or instrument response, keep the H2O donor comparison as the primary test and report isotope arms as bounded descriptive comparisons only.

Limitations: This card is intentionally narrowed to a descriptive covariate screen. TEA and TEOA may simultaneously alter proton transfer, reductive quenching, coordination, viscosity, and CO2 solvation. Bulk acidity and conductivity do not establish the local environment at active sites, and D2O is not a single-factor perturbation. Therefore the experiment can prioritize which explanations deserve stronger follow-up, but it cannot by itself prove proton-management causation.

Feasibility: low_cost

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This remains a defensible descriptive covariate screen. The card now focuses on directly measured bulk descriptors that first pass a stability pilot in the actual medium, prioritizing one consistent acidity proxy and conductivity, and it repeatedly states that these are bulk covariates rather than direct measures of the local catalytic environment. The matched TEA/TEOA and H2O/D2O comparisons can therefore test whether donor-dependent catalytic differences co-vary with stable measurable medium descriptors, while keeping the conclusion descriptive instead of causal. The previously problematic overreach around CO2-uptake proxy has been removed from the core design, and the D2O arms are already bounded as isotope perturbations rather than single-factor mechanistic proof. With those limits preserved, no major issue blocks shortlist.

H04

Claim: Changing TEA to TEOA alters cobalt speciation, coordination environment, support association, or deactivation pathways during turnover, and this state change contributes to the CO/H2 difference. A discriminating prediction is that donor substitution yields different in situ or near-operando cobalt-state signatures or stability trends that correlate with catalytic output, whereas indistinguishable cobalt-state observables would weaken this explanation.

Alternative: The active cobalt state and deactivation behavior are effectively the same with TEA and TEOA, so product differences originate upstream in donor photochemistry or downstream in proton/CO2 handling instead of cobalt speciation.

Platform and comparator choice: P01 remains primary because the question is about the original Co-bpy context and supported g-C3N4 formulation; P02 can supplement if a homogeneous cobalt system gives cleaner donor-dependent state readouts. The comparator remains single-variable TEA-for-TEOA substitution within a fixed baseline.

Uncertainty: The exact active cobalt species in P01 is unresolved even before donor substitution, so state assignments may remain ambiguous. Much of the coordination precedent comes from other cobalt families or other metals, limiting mechanistic transfer.

E19 | Operando cobalt-state comparison under TEOA versus TEA

Question: Test whether replacing TEOA with TEA in the primary P01 Co-bpy/g-C3N4 CO2-photoreduction formulation changes observable cobalt oxidation-state, coordination, or stability signatures during operation, and whether any donor-dependent state difference tracks catalytic output.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV059 anchors the P01 TEOA-based CoCl2+bpy+g-C3N4 formulation and motivates TEA as a comparator within the same family. EV052, EV076, EV088, GX008, and EV068 support operando or near-operando XAS plus complementary UV-vis/Raman/IR style characterization as a way to monitor catalyst state during CO2 reduction rather than relying only on recovered samples. GX001, GX005, and GX011 justify testing whether donor identity could alter cobalt coordination/speciation, but these are transferred from other cobalt or bipyridine-related systems and do not establish the same chemistry here. EV085 cautions that donor decomposition or decomposition-derived catalysts can confound interpretation, so this proposal is limited to donor-dependent cobalt-linked observables and stability trends unless independent methods converge.

EV052, EV076, EV088, GX008, EV059, GX001, GX005, GX011, EV085, EV068

Supports: Support for H04 would be a reproducible donor-dependent difference between E19G01 and E19G02 in cobalt-linked operando or near-operando observables under otherwise matched conditions, with TEA and TEOA showing different spectral evolution trajectories, coordination trends, or decay patterns that align with different CO/H2 outputs, while the paired no-cobalt matrices do not account for the differences.

Refutes: Evidence against H04 would be that E19G01 and E19G02 show indistinguishable cobalt-linked spectral features and temporal evolution within method resolution, similar stability trends, and no donor-dependent divergence beyond matrix backgrounds, despite independently reproducible CO/H2 differences under the same matched conditions. That would weaken cobalt-speciation or cobalt-deactivation as the main explanation.

Inconclusive: The result is inconclusive if cobalt signals are too weak, suspension/scattering prevents reliable extraction, donor substitution changes matrix background enough to obscure cobalt features even after no-cobalt controls, multiple cobalt-state models fit equally well, or product differences are not reproduced under the exact spectroscopic-cell conditions. A donor-dependent spectral change without product correlation remains descriptive only.

Parameter selection: Do not invent numerical loadings or beam conditions. Use the literature-anchored P01 composition only after source verification. Pilot first to choose the lowest cobalt concentration and path length that still give reproducible cobalt-edge signal above both donor-matrix backgrounds while preserving measurable CO/H2 formation in the spectroscopic cell. Match donor formulations by the same verified baseline recipe except donor identity. Calibrate illumination by equal reactor geometry and measured incident photon flux at the sample. If true operando XAS is impractical, downgrade to near-operando rapid-transfer sampling with the same groups and explicitly narrow inference to time-adjacent, not strictly operating-state, observations.

Limitations: The active cobalt species in P01 is unresolved, and support-associated, solution-phase, and decomposition-derived cobalt species may coexist. Operando XAS is element-specific but cannot by itself prove the catalytic intermediate, isolate donor photochemistry as upstream cause, or exclude decomposition pathways. Complementary UV-vis changes are non-unique and may reflect donor, ligand, support, or scattering effects. Dark comparisons here remain descriptive because irradiation and sampling_mode both differ. Even with paired no-cobalt matrices, assignment should stop at donor-dependent cobalt-linked observables unless independent methods support a narrower claim.

Feasibility: specialist

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This re-review resolves the prior control and claim-scope problems. The card now includes symmetric no-cobalt illuminated references for both donors (E19G05 and E19G06), directly addressing the earlier missing TEA background issue. It also explicitly limits inference to donor-dependent cobalt-linked observables, stability trends, and correlation with output, while warning that dark comparisons are descriptive and that species naming remains model-dependent unless independent observables converge. That bounded scope is appropriate because EV059 directly anchors the P01 formulation family, while EV052, EV076, EV088, GX008, and EV068 support operando or near-operando state-tracking methods only as transferable methodology. GX001, GX005, and GX011 remain transferred mechanistic motivation rather than direct proof for this exact chemistry, but the proposed experiment now treats them cautiously. As revised, this is a defensible high-value researcher candidate for matched donor substitution in the primary system.

E20 | TEA versus TEOA pre-irradiation association with Co-bpy formulations

Question: Test whether replacing TEOA with TEA changes detectable pre-turnover association or coordination signatures of the Co-bpy formulation, which could predispose different catalytic states before photochemistry begins.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV059 anchors the target CoCl2+bpy, g-C3N4, MeCN/H2O, TEOA formulation family. GX005 shows spectrophotometric titration can detect axial coordination in a cobalt system, GX001 and EV048 motivate donor/coordination competition and 1H NMR titration as a transferable comparison tool. The proposal transfers these methods cautiously to ask only whether TEA and TEOA produce different pre-irradiation observables, not whether any observed adduct is the active catalytic species.

GX001, GX005, EV048, EV059

Supports: Support for H04 would be reproducible donor-dependent pre-irradiation observables under otherwise matched conditions, especially if TEA and TEOA give different shift patterns, different apparent association onset or saturation behavior, or one donor perturbs the Co-bpy precursor while the other does not. Concordant TEA-versus-TEOA differences in both the P01 precursor mixture and the simpler homogeneous analogue would strengthen transfer to cobalt-centered preassociation rather than a support-only artifact.

Refutes: H04 is weakened if TEA and TEOA give indistinguishable spectra within experimental resolution across matched titrations, while donor-free controls remain stable and the analogue also shows no donor-specific difference. That outcome would argue against strong pre-turnover donor-specific cobalt association as the main source of the CO/H2 difference, though it would not exclude differences arising later under irradiation.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if spectra are dominated by scattering from g-C3N4, if cobalt/bpy precursor instability masks titration responses, if only one method is interpretable, or if both donors alter bulk medium properties without a cobalt-specific signature. Any observed shift also remains non-diagnostic for the catalytically active state because pre-irradiation association need not persist during turnover.

Parameter selection: Use the literature-defined P01 composition family without inventing missing numerical values. First run a pilot with donor-free precursor to identify a time window over which baseline drift is acceptably small. Choose amine addition increments that span from no visible spectral change to either a reproducible plateau or the onset of non-ideal behavior such as precipitation; use the same increment scheme for TEA and TEOA. Attempt 1H NMR only on simplified homogeneous analogue samples that remain sufficiently resolved; if the P01 slurry is not NMR-interpretable, restrict NMR to the analogue and keep UV-Vis as the common measurement. If apparent binding-model fitting is unstable or non-unique, report descriptive titration trends instead of forcing equilibrium constants.

Limitations: Most evidence is transferred from other cobalt families or other metals, so any assignment to direct cobalt coordination is provisional. P01 contains g-C3N4, so scattering and heterogeneous interfaces may obscure weak spectral changes. TEA and TEOA also change medium properties, so a spectral difference does not uniquely prove inner-sphere coordination. Conversely, no detectable change does not rule out transient or photoinduced interactions below detection limits. This characterization cannot establish product origin, catalytic necessity, or the identity of the true operating species under illumination.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This remains a well-bounded pre-irradiation characterization candidate. EV059 provides the closest direct anchor for the precursor formulation family, while GX005, GX001, and EV048 support only the transferable plausibility of donor-sensitive association measurements and titration-style comparisons. The design asks a narrow question: whether TEA and TEOA generate different pre-turnover observables in matched precursor mixtures, with donor-free reference and a simpler homogeneous analogue for cleaner assignment. The card does not overclaim that any observed association is the active catalyst, and it explicitly treats scattering, instability, and medium-property changes as limitations. Because the inferential target is modest and the controls are coherent, this remains defensible for researcher consideration.

  "refutes": "H04 is weakened if TEA and TEOA give indistinguishable spectra within experimental resolution across matched titrations, while donor-free controls remain stable and the analogue also shows no donor-specific difference. That outcome would argue against strong pre-turnover donor-specific cobalt association as the main source of the CO/H2 difference, though it would not exclude differences arising later under irradiation.",
  "inconclusive": "Results are inconclusive if spectra are dominated by scattering from g-C3N4, if cobalt/bpy precursor instability masks titration responses, if only one method is interpretable, or if both donors alter bulk medium properties without a cobalt-specific signature. Any observed shift also remains non-diagnostic for the catalytically active state because pre-irradiation association need not persist during turnover.",
  "parameter_selection": "Use the literature-defined P01 composition family without inventing missing numerical values. First run a pilot with donor-free precursor to identify a time window over which baseline drift is acceptably small. Choose amine addition increments that span from no visible spectral change to either a reproducible plateau or the onset of non-ideal behavior such as precipitation; use the same increment scheme for TEA and TEOA. Attempt 1H NMR only on simplified homogeneous analogue samples that remain sufficiently resolved; if the P01 slurry is not NMR-interpretable, restrict NMR to the analogue and keep UV-Vis as the common measurement. If apparent binding-model fitting is unstable or non-unique, report descriptive titration trends instead of forcing equilibrium constants.",
  "limitations": "Most evidence is transferred from other cobalt families or other metals, so any assignment to direct cobalt coordination is provisional. TEA and TEOA also change medium properties, so a spectral difference does not uniquely prove inner-sphere coordination. Conversely, no detectable change does not rule out transient or photoinduced interactions below detection limits. This characterization cannot establish product origin, catalytic necessity, or the identity of the true operating species under illumination.",

E21 | Deactivation and catalyst persistence map

Question: Test whether replacing TEOA with TEA changes time-dependent cobalt persistence, donor-dependent state drift, or loss of catalytic output in the primary Co-bpy/g-C3N4 platform, rather than only changing endpoint selectivity.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV059 anchors the primary CoCl2+bpy/g-C3N4/MeCN-H2O/TEOA platform and motivates a single-variable TEA comparator. EV051 and EV085 justify treating donor identity as a possible driver of catalyst stability, decomposition, or donor-derived confounding rather than assuming unchanged active species. EV004 and GX008 support paired operando or near-operando state tracking with catalytic time-course and rigorous controls. Proposed transfer: apply matched donor substitution within P01 and compare decay profiles and cobalt-state observables over time; this is a proposal, not a reported literature result for this exact system.

EV051, EV085, EV004, GX008, EV059

Supports: Support for H04 would be a reproducible donor-dependent difference between E21G01 and E21G02 in catalytic decay profile, cobalt persistence, or cobalt-state trajectory that is absent or much smaller in the no-cobalt controls. Stronger support is obtained if the donor giving different CO/H2 behavior also shows distinct cobalt-state drift or faster loss of cobalt-associated signal over time under otherwise matched conditions.

Refutes: H04 is weakened if E21G01 and E21G02 show similar time-dependent CO and H2 evolution, similar catalyst persistence, and no reproducible donor-dependent difference in cobalt-state observables beyond measurement uncertainty, while no-cobalt controls remain low and uninformative. It is further weakened if donor-dependent catalytic differences occur without any detectable difference in cobalt-state or persistence despite adequate sensitivity.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if donor substitution changes multiple uncontrolled medium properties, if characterization sensitivity is insufficient to resolve cobalt under operating conditions, if no-cobalt controls show substantial donor-dependent background products or overlapping signals, or if only ex situ post-reaction differences are observed without validated linkage to the operating state.

Parameter selection: Do not invent numeric settings. Use the source-anchored P01 formulation as the starting point and verify any missing concentrations, irradiation range, and sampling interval from the supplied source record before execution. Pilot rules: first confirm product signals are above blank and within the linear range of the product assay; choose time points dense enough to detect an early induction period, plateau, or decay; choose spectroscopic acquisition settings on concentrated matched samples or extended integration only after confirming that the method resolves cobalt above donor/support background; if operando signal is too weak, switch to a validated rapid-transfer near-operando workflow rather than claiming operating-state identity.

Limitations: Direct literature evidence for a strict TEA-versus-TEOA head-to-head in this exact P01 recipe is incomplete. The active cobalt species is unresolved, so state changes may remain descriptive rather than structurally unique. TEA and TEOA can alter donor strength, proton handling, CO2 interaction, viscosity, and coordination simultaneously, so even the primary contrast does not isolate a single causal subfactor. No-cobalt controls bound some background chemistry but do not recreate the full catalytic manifold. Post-reaction characterization alone cannot establish the true operating state.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This remains a strong candidate because it focuses on donor-dependent decay profiles, catalyst persistence, and time-dependent cobalt-state drift rather than unique structural assignment. EV059 anchors the primary system; EV085 and EV051 justify examining donor-linked decomposition or stability effects; EV004 and GX008 support paired time-course characterization with controls as a transferable methodological approach. The design includes symmetric no-cobalt controls for both donors, which is important for excluding donor/support background products or signals. The card also appropriately limits conclusions: different decay or cobalt-state trajectories would support a cobalt-state or deactivation contribution, not mechanistic proof. That inferential framing is well matched to the supplied evidence.

E22 | Operando IR/EPR donor-adduct screen

Question: Test whether TEA versus TEOA causes different donor-associated cobalt or substrate-bound species under matched P01 conditions, while bounding what any spectral differences can and cannot establish about the CO/H2 shift.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: GX011 suggests TEOA can form catalyst adduct-like species in a bipyridine carbonyl system, motivating a cautious transfer test for donor-specific adduct chemistry in Co-bpy rather than assuming the same species. EV016 shows EPR can detect TEOA-derived radicals and that TEA/TEOA can differ in line shape because of physical properties, supporting donor-comparative EPR with matched blanks. EV068 supports operando FTIR/EPR to follow working-state interactions instead of recovered samples only. EV085 warns that donor decomposition and decomposition-derived active species can confound assignments, so symmetric donor-specific blanks are required and any signal must be treated as descriptive unless tied to matched catalytic outputs.

GX011, EV068, EV016, EV085

Supports: H04 is supported if, under the locked matched P01 comparison, TEA and TEOA show reproducibly different cobalt-requiring and light-dependent IR and or EPR signatures, or different persistence or loss of such signatures over time, and those differences track a donor-dependent CO/H2 change better than donor-free, no-cobalt, or dark backgrounds do.

Refutes: H04 is weakened if TEA and TEOA give no reproducible difference in cobalt-requiring, light-dependent IR/EPR observables within detection limits while catalytic CO/H2 still differs under the same matched conditions, favoring explanations upstream in donor photochemistry or downstream in proton or CO2 handling rather than cobalt-state divergence.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if spectra are too weak, dominated by background or support signals, changed by viscosity or other physical effects without cobalt specificity, or if donor-dependent signals appear but cannot be distinguished from donor decomposition, generic radical formation, or unsupported assignments. Correlation without clean symmetric controls also remains inconclusive.

Parameter selection: Use the literature-backed P01 formulation family only for component identity, not exact invented numbers. First verify the baseline recipe from source records. Then run pilots to set sample concentration, path length, irradiation interval, and EPR acquisition mode by the rule that cobalt-containing samples must show stable unsaturated baselines and donor-free and no-cobalt controls must remain interpretable. If TEA and TEOA differ strongly in viscosity or optical density, adjust only the measurement configuration, not the chemical formulation, and document that this preserves matched chemistry while changing detection settings. Choose time points by a calibration run that captures pre-illumination, early illumination, and a later turnover window without exhausting the sample.

Limitations: Assignments are transferred from nonidentical systems and may not map to Co-bpy. IR/EPR can reveal donor-dependent species or state changes but cannot alone establish the full catalytic mechanism, the exact structure of the active cobalt species, or product origin. TEA/TEOA physical-property differences can alter line shape and transport, so apparent spectral differences are not automatically chemical. Near-operando aliquots may miss the true instantaneous working state. No-cobalt controls are background screens, not full single-factor catalytic comparisons because total composition changes.

Feasibility: specialist

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - The prior asymmetry problem has been fixed. The card now includes no-cobalt controls for both donors (E22G04, E22G05) and dark controls for both donors (E22G06, E22G07), directly addressing the earlier weakness in donor-specific interpretation. It also now frames the study as an operando or near-operando IR/EPR screen with conservative assignment rules, explicitly warning that features may arise from donor radicals, viscosity effects, decomposition, or background signals rather than named cobalt intermediates. That is important because the evidence base is mainly transferred: GX011 motivates possible TEOA adduct chemistry only in a different metal system, EV016 supports donor-comparative EPR and highlights physical-property effects, EV068 supports operando methods broadly, and EV085 warns about decomposition confounds. With symmetric controls and a descriptive screen-level scope, this is now defensible for researcher consideration even though assignment confidence may remain limited.

E23 | P02 donor-substitution bridge for cobalt-state and quenching readouts

Question: Test in the supplementary homogeneous cobalt platform whether replacing TEOA with TEA changes cobalt-state-sensitive observables or donor-coupled quenching behavior under otherwise matched conditions, then compare only the qualitative direction of donor effects to the primary P01 system.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV065 supports a defined homogeneous cobalt photocatalytic platform with TEOA and measurable quenching; EV053 supports using fluorescence/UV-vis to distinguish donor-versus-catalyst quenching routes; EV052 and GX008 support operando or near-operando state tracking plus negative controls. Proposed transfer is limited: this is a cleaner bridge assay, not the target Co-bpy/g-C3N4 chemistry, so agreement would support donor-linked state trends while disagreement could reflect platform-specific speciation.

EV065, EV053, EV052, GX008

Supports: Support for H04 would be a reproducible donor-dependent change between E23G01 and E23G02 in cobalt-associated spectral or operando XAS observables that is absent or much smaller in the corresponding cobalt-free blanks, with the qualitative donor trend aligning with a donor-linked change in CO/H2 output. Agreement in direction with analogous P01 observations would strengthen transfer, while remaining bounded to trend-level rather than identity-level claims.

Refutes: H04 is weakened if E23G01 and E23G02 show indistinguishable cobalt-dependent spectra and quenching-corrected state readouts across the operando window, while catalytic differences persist or are explained by donor-only blank behavior. It is also weakened if apparent donor differences arise mainly in E23G03 versus E23G04, indicating upstream donor/photosensitizer effects without clear cobalt-state divergence.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if donor substitution changes optical baselines, viscosity, solubility, or inner-filter behavior enough to prevent matched interpretation; if cobalt signals are below detection or dominated by broad unresolved features; if TEA and TEOA require materially different concentration windows to keep the assay stable; or if P02 shows donor effects but P01 comparison data are unavailable for bridging.

Parameter selection: Use the literature-defined P02 component identities and solvent family, but verify exact concentrations and illumination settings from the supplied source before execution. If any setting is still uncertain, choose it by pilot rules: maintain measurable CO/H2 within a short assay window, keep absorbance/emission in the instrument linear range, keep cobalt and photosensitizer solutions visibly homogeneous, and use one common setting for both donors whenever possible. If one donor destabilizes the mixture under the common setting, reduce all concentrations proportionally and re-run the full matched set rather than optimizing donors separately.

Limitations: Catalyst architecture differs from the Co-bpy/g-C3N4 target, so this is a transfer test rather than a direct explanation of P01. Donor substitution can change medium properties and quenching simultaneously, and homogeneous average-state methods may not reveal minority active species. Cobalt-free blanks help bound but do not eliminate confounding. Spectral changes alone cannot establish exact coordination structures or prove causality for product selectivity.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - This remains a reasonable supplementary bridge candidate. EV065 supports a defined homogeneous cobalt photocatalytic platform with TEOA and measurable quenching behavior, while EV053 supports quenching-pathway discrimination by fluorescence and UV-vis as a transferable mechanistic method. The card explicitly limits transfer: P02 is a cleaner auxiliary platform, not a substitute for the P01 Co-bpy/g-C3N4 chemistry. The donor-specific cobalt-free blanks help separate donor/photosensitizer background effects from cobalt-dependent observables. Because the proposed inference is trend-level and bounded, agreement or disagreement with P01 would both be informative without overclaiming mechanistic identity.

E24 | Single-omission matrix for donor-linked cobalt-state effects

Question: Test whether any TEA-versus-TEOA difference in cobalt-state observables and CO/H2 distribution requires the full CoCl2+bpy+g-C3N4 assembly, using explicit one-factor omissions and bounded background interpretation.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV059 supports the CoCl2+bpy+g-C3N4+TEOA formulation family in MeCN/H2O and motivates this as the closest source-anchored platform for donor comparison. GX008 supports explicit negative controls and paired characterization plus product analysis. EV081 shows that partial or no-catalyst systems can still produce CO and H2, so omission results must be treated as necessity/background tests rather than proof of mechanism. GX016 supports the transferable idea that cobalt-centered identity can shift product selectivity under TEOA photocatalysis, motivating tests of whether donor-linked effects persist or collapse when ligand or support is removed. The proposal transfers these observations into a controlled omission matrix in the P01-style system; it does not assume literature-established active-species identity or that omitted formulations are mechanistic continuations of the full catalyst.

EV059, GX016, GX008, EV081

Supports: H04 is supported if the full assembly shows a reproducible TEA-versus-TEOA difference in cobalt-state observables or stability trends and that donor-linked difference weakens, disappears, or changes character in at least one omission pair, indicating dependence on ligand or support context. Stronger support occurs if the direction of cobalt-state differences tracks the direction of CO/H2 changes across matched pairs.

Refutes: H04 is weakened if TEA and TEOA give indistinguishable cobalt-state and stability observables in the full assembly within method resolution, and the ligand-omission and support-omission donor pairs likewise show no reproducible donor-linked divergence despite measurable products. It is further weakened if donor substitution changes CO/H2 but cobalt-state observables remain unchanged across all matched formulations.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if product signals are near background, cobalt-state signals are below detection or assignment is non-unique, omission causes optical or compositional changes that prevent comparable measurements, or session-to-session drift obscures donor-pair comparisons. A change seen in only one method without corroboration remains provisional.

Parameter selection: Do not invent concentrations, catalyst loadings, or illumination settings. Use the verified EV059-style baseline for starting composition, then perform a pilot to choose the lowest concentration and path length that still provide stable cobalt-state signal above blanks and measurable CO and H2 above analytical detection. Choose an early sampling window before heavy conversion or visible precipitation. If support omission changes turbidity or absorbance, recalibrate acquisition settings with blanks while keeping reactor geometry, gas handling, and timing identical across compared groups.

Limitations: Omission of bpy or g-C3N4 may generate qualitatively different cobalt species rather than partial versions of the full catalyst, so these are dependence tests, not continuity proofs. EV059 does not provide a direct TEA-versus-TEOA head-to-head in one frozen recipe, so baseline comparability still requires verification. Recovered-sample measurements may miss the operating state, and generic spectral changes cannot by themselves identify the active species or exact coordination geometry.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - The prior structural problem has been resolved. The support-omission arm is now split into explicit TEOA and TEA groups (E24G05 and E24G06), so donor comparisons within each omission state are cleanly encoded, and the main donor benchmark in the full assembly is also explicit. The card also clearly states that omission systems are dependence or necessity tests rather than mechanistic continuity tests, which addresses the earlier concern about overinterpreting qualitatively different cobalt chemistry after ligand or support removal. EV059 anchors the baseline formulation family; GX008 supports omission controls and paired characterization; EV081 cautions that omission or no-catalyst systems can still make products; GX016 supports the general idea that cobalt-centered identity can influence product selectivity. The evidence is still transfer-limited, but the revised comparison structure and bounded interpretation make this a defensible candidate for researcher planning.

Researcher decision: [ ] Select [ ] Modify before selection [ ] Defer

H05

Claim: TEOA, more than TEA, may participate non-innocently in substrate-associated chemistry by forming amine- or catalyst-linked CO2 adducts or stabilizing intermediates, thereby changing apparent selectivity without requiring a different primary quenching step. A discriminating prediction is that donor substitution changes substrate- or intermediate-associated spectroscopic signatures or CO2-origin-bounded behavior even when electron-supply observables are similar.

Alternative: No donor-specific substrate adduct or intermediate stabilization is important, and any TEA/TEOA selectivity difference is fully explained by quenching kinetics, cobalt-state changes, or uncontrolled background reactions.

Platform and comparator choice: P01 is adequate because it keeps the Co-bpy context while allowing donor substitution under one baseline; the key comparison is TEA versus TEOA with matched CO2 atmosphere and medium. Supplementary transfer to P02 is lower value here because homogeneous donor-side quenching there does not directly test substrate-associated adduct chemistry.

Uncertainty: Direct observation of such intermediates in a TEA/TEOA Co-bpy comparison is absent from the supplied data, so this is a falsifiable transfer hypothesis rather than an established mechanism. Some related claims are author interpretations and may not generalize across metals or solvents.

E25 | CO2-associated speciation versus donor identity before illumination

Question: Test whether matched P01 mixtures containing TEA or TEOA show different pre-illumination CO2-associated solution signatures, and whether any donor-dependent signal requires cobalt/bipyridine presence. This addresses the H05 prediction of donor-linked substrate or intermediate association without presuming catalytic relevance.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: Sources report or interpret TEOA as capable of CO2 trapping or catalyst-adduct chemistry in non-cobalt systems and show that medium can alter observable intermediates; this proposal transfers only the testable prediction that donor identity may change observable CO2-associated states under matched Co-bpy conditions. Operando/isotopic assignment evidence is used only to justify control logic, not to assume identical species in P01.

EV029, GX011, EV031, EV061, EV021, EV080, GX008

Supports: Support for H05 would be reproducible CO2-dependent signatures that differ between TEA and TEOA under otherwise matched P01 conditions, especially if TEOA shows additional or stronger features absent in inert controls and not reproduced by the no-amine CO2 sample. Stronger support would come from consistent assignment logic across two observables, such as IR plus NMR, while still remaining descriptive rather than mechanistic proof.

Refutes: H05 is weakened if TEA and TEOA give no reproducible difference in any CO2-dependent pre-illumination signature under matched conditions, or if all apparent donor differences disappear after background subtraction, replicate preparation, and gas-control checks. It is also weakened if observed features are equally present under inert gas, indicating donor/background rather than CO2-associated origin.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if signals are too weak, unresolved, or irreproducible; if TEA and TEOA differ but only alongside uncontrolled pH, water-content, or solubility changes; or if spectra show donor-dependent bulk amine-CO2 speciation but cannot separate that from catalyst-associated adducts. A null result does not exclude transient light-induced differences.

Parameter selection: Do not invent concentrations or acquisition settings. Use the verified P01 baseline composition from supplied evidence. Run a short pilot to set gas-equilibration time, sample concentration window, and spectral averaging so that major solvent and donor backgrounds are stable and not detector-saturating. If both IR and NMR are attempted, choose the method giving reproducible CO2-minus-inert difference signals first; add the second method only if it offers independent assignment value. If signal overlap prevents interpretation, prioritize isotopic or background controls only after basic detectability is confirmed.

Limitations: This experiment is characterization only and cannot establish that any detected species lies on the catalytic pathway or controls CO/H2 selectivity. TEA/TEOA change multiple chemical properties beyond simple donor strength, so donor identity is a bundled factor even in the single-factor contrast. Because cobalt, bpy, and g-C3N4 remain present in all main groups, any observed signal may arise from bulk amine-CO2 chemistry, catalyst-linked adducts, support-associated species, or combinations thereof. Pre-illumination measurements may miss the operating photochemical state.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - E25 remains a defensible descriptive characterization candidate. It directly tests the stated H05 prediction that TEA versus TEOA may alter pre-illumination CO2-associated speciation under otherwise matched P01 conditions, and it uses appropriate bounding controls: CO2 versus inert gas plus a no-amine condition. The proposal is explicit that any observed difference would remain descriptive and could reflect bulk amine-CO2 speciation, catalyst-linked adducts, support-associated species, or mixtures. That limited claim matches the supplied evidence, which is transferred from other systems rather than direct Co-bpy proof. Because the experiment does not overclaim catalytic relevance or exact structure, the inference is adequate for the narrower observational question.
