E26 | Operando isotope-sensitive IR for TEA versus TEOA

Question: Test whether donor identity changes reaction-state IR features consistent with CO2-associated or hydrogen-involving species in the P01 Co-bpy context, using symmetric isotope and negative controls so any TEA-versus-TEOA difference is bounded in both donor conditions rather than inferred from products alone.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV080 and GX008 support operando IR with isotope and negative controls as a transferable strategy. EV031 and EV029 motivate the narrower proposal that donor identity can alter intermediate populations or CO2-associated adduct chemistry, but they do not establish the same species in Co-bpy. This revised card therefore compares TEA and TEOA under matched conditions and applies parallel assignment controls on both donor sides where feasible.

EV080, EV031, EV029, GX008

Supports: Support for H05 would be a reproducible TEA-versus-TEOA difference in reaction-state IR features under otherwise matched conditions, with at least one donor-dependent feature showing internally consistent isotope behavior and cobalt dependence on the relevant donor side. The strongest support is when analogous assignment controls bound TEA and TEOA features differently, consistent with donor-specific CO2-associated or hydrogen-involving chemistry rather than simple background differences.

Refutes: Evidence against H05 would be that TEA and TEOA show no reproducible donor-dependent reaction-state IR differences beyond baseline noise or generic medium changes, or that apparent differences disappear once symmetric isotope and cobalt-absent controls are applied. That outcome would favor explanations centered elsewhere, such as quenching kinetics, cobalt-state changes not resolved here, or uncontrolled catalytic variability.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if spectra are dominated by overlap, weak signals, or baseline drift; if D2O or 13CO2 causes broad perturbation without interpretable assignment value; if only one donor condition yields usable isotope-control data; or if donor-dependent bands cannot be separated from cobalt-free backgrounds. In that case the experiment remains descriptive and cannot cleanly discriminate H05 from competing explanations.

Parameter selection: Do not invent literature conditions. Use the source-verified P01 formulation where available; otherwise first pilot each donor condition separately to find the highest concentration, path length, and collection time that preserve unsaturated absorbance and stable baselines. Keep reactor geometry, illumination, gas handling, and acquisition timing identical across TEA and TEOA. Apply 13CO2 and D2O only after the corresponding unlabeled donor condition gives reproducible transient bands. If D2O destabilizes one donor formulation or causes severe rate collapse, retain that arm as a bounded assignment aid and narrow inference accordingly rather than treating TEA and TEOA as fully matched.

Limitations: Evidence is transferred from non-Co systems and general methods. Band assignment may remain ambiguous because donor, solvent, bicarbonate or carbonate, products, and cobalt-ligand features can overlap. Isotope substitution can alter kinetics as well as frequencies. Near-operando spectra may miss the true operating state, and operando IR alone cannot prove the primary quenching route, exact adduct identity, or whether a detected species is on-path rather than spectator. Cobalt-absent controls bound background contributions but do not separately isolate all non-cobalt components.

Feasibility: specialist

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - E26 remains defensible for researcher consideration because the revised design preserves the key strengths identified previously: matched TEA and TEOA operando runs, paired 13CO2 controls on both donor sides, paired D2O substitutions where feasible on both donor sides, and cobalt-absent references for both donors. This symmetric control structure materially improves assignment logic relative to a one-sided screen. The card also correctly limits interpretation: donor-dependent IR differences can indicate a donor-linked change in the reacting ensemble, but they do not uniquely prove CO2 adduct chemistry or exclude altered cobalt state or broader medium effects. The evidence base is still transferred method support rather than direct Co-bpy mechanistic evidence, but for a specialist operando screen with bounded conclusions, the inferential scope is appropriate.

E27 | 13CO2-bounded TEA versus TEOA benchmark with matched background controls

Question: Test whether any TEA/TEOA difference in apparent CO/H2 selectivity remains when counted CO is explicitly confirmed as CO2-derived within the P01 Co-bpy comparison, while checking donor-dependent background gas formation under no-CO2 and dark conditions.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV021 motivates explicit isotope origin-bounding because CO was reported from CO2 rather than TEOA decomposition in a different TEOA-containing photochemical system. EV085 warns that donor decomposition and altered active species can confound product assignment. GX008 supports negative controls and reproducible 13CO2 labeling in photocatalytic CO2-reduction studies. EV063 shows that in a CoCl2/bpy-containing photocatalytic system, CO and H2 outputs are medium-sensitive, so the TEA/TEOA comparison should keep all non-donor factors matched. Proposed transfer: apply isotope-bounded product analysis to the matched P01 donor substitution; this bounds carbon-origin alternatives but does not directly identify adducts or operating cobalt states.

EV021, EV085, GX008, EV063

Supports: Support for H05 is stronger if G01 and G02 show a reproducible donor-dependent difference in the CO2-derived 13CO fraction or 13CO/H2 ratio while inert-gas controls remain low for donor-derived background CO and the broader program finds no commensurate donor difference in upstream electron-supply observables. This would show that the TEA/TEOA effect is not merely an artifact of counting non-CO2 carbon as product, though it would still not prove adduct identity.

Refutes: H05 is weakened if the apparent TEA/TEOA CO difference disappears once analysis is restricted to confirmed 13CO2-derived CO, or if donor-dependent CO appears similarly in inert-gas or dark controls, indicating that background chemistry can account for the prior selectivity difference. H05 is also weakened if 13CO/H2 remains indistinguishable between donors under well-matched conditions and controls.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if isotopic resolution is insufficient, baseline CO contamination or carryover is non-negligible, medium matching between TEA and TEOA is not credible, or any observed donor effect is comparable to run-to-run variability. A retained donor difference in 13CO origin confirms a real product-origin effect but still cannot distinguish adduct stabilization from cobalt-state or quenching explanations.

Parameter selection: Do not invent concentrations, light dose, or runtime. Use the verified P01 baseline if explicitly available; otherwise run a pilot to choose a reaction duration that gives quantifiable CO and H2 above blank yet remains before obvious saturation or severe deactivation. Calibrate gas analysis with labeled and unlabeled CO standards or equivalent instrument checks, confirm no memory effect by blank injections, and use the same sampling cadence and reactor volume across all groups. If TEA substitution changes pH, conductivity, or phase behavior enough to break comparability, document this and either predefine a matched-medium adjustment pilot or treat the comparison as partially confounded rather than a strict single-factor test.

Limitations: This candidate bounds carbon origin but does not directly observe amine-CO2 adducts, catalyst-linked intermediates, or operating cobalt speciation. TEA and TEOA can change multiple hidden properties even when nominal formulation is matched, including proton activity and solvation, so a donor effect here is not uniquely attributable to adduct stabilization. The dark control is intentionally limited and, if only one donor is used, becomes a multi-factor descriptive check rather than a donor-specific contrast. Transfer from the cited literature is methodological and cautionary, not direct proof for P01.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - E27 remains a good bounded candidate because it asks a narrower, important question: whether any apparent TEA/TEOA difference in CO/H2 selectivity persists when counted CO is explicitly confirmed as CO2-derived and donor-dependent background gas formation is checked. The 13CO2 head-to-head comparison is aligned with the stated hypothesis as an origin-bounding product test, and the inert-gas plus dark controls appropriately bound non-CO2 and non-photochemical gas formation. The card correctly avoids claiming that isotope-bounded product origin identifies adducts or operating cobalt states. The supporting evidence is transferred and cautionary rather than direct for P01, but that is sufficient for this limited product-origin question.

E28 | TEA/TEOA matched catalytic readout with inert-gas, negative controls, and optional isotope follow-up

Question: Test whether any TEA-versus-TEOA difference in CO/H2 distribution persists after tighter matching plus core controls, while limiting inference to a bounded donor comparison unless a platform-qualified substrate-associated signal is first established.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV011 supports the competing explanation that both TEA and TEOA can function through reductive quenching and donor-radical formation. EV029 and GX011 provide transferable, non-Co evidence that TEOA can participate in catalyst- or CO2-associated adduct chemistry beyond simple donation. GX008 supports inclusion of omitted-component controls, inert-gas comparison, and 13CO2 checks in homogeneous photocatalytic CO2-reduction studies. Proposed transfer: in the researcher-stated Co(II)-bipyridine comparison platform, first establish whether a TEA/TEOA catalytic difference is reproducible under tightly matched conditions and core controls, then add one substrate-associated spectroscopic readout only if a pilot shows it is detectable and comparable in this platform. This is a proposal, not a reported Co-bpy result.

EV029, GX011, EV011, GX008

Supports: Support for H05 would be a reproducible TEA-versus-TEOA difference in CO/H2 distribution under the matched donor comparison that remains after cobalt, dark, and inert-gas controls validate the assay, together with quenching observables that remain similar within a predeclared repeatability window and, if available, a donor-dependent substrate-associated or intermediate-associated signal. Consistent 13CO2 product origin would strengthen interpretation but would still not by itself establish a specific adduct structure.

Refutes: Evidence against H05 would be that the TEA/TEOA catalytic difference disappears under stricter matching, or that any remaining catalytic difference is accompanied by clearly different quenching behavior large enough to account for it, or that CO/H2 signals are reproduced in inert-gas, no-cobalt, or dark controls in ways that undermine a cobalt-linked donor-specific interpretation.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive if quenching and downstream-sensitive observables both change together, if the substrate-associated method cannot be qualified in this platform, if products are near detection limits, if isotope follow-up is below sensitivity, or if donor substitution changes bulk medium properties enough that multiple latent factors vary despite nominal matching.

Parameter selection: Do not invent concentrations or irradiation settings. Use the researcher-stated Co-bpy baseline values only if explicitly verified in the supplied material; otherwise perform a pilot to define a workable window where both donors give measurable but not overloaded product signals and where the chosen quenching assay is above background. Operationalize 'similar quenching' before the main run as no donor difference larger than the empirically determined repeatability bound from replicate baseline measurements with the same assay and sample state. Add the substrate-associated module only if the pilot shows a reproducible signal in both donor conditions without uncontrolled precipitation or severe overlap.

Limitations: The card remains a staged discriminator because the supplied record does not establish a validated substrate-associated signal in the target platform. TEA and TEOA can alter several medium properties beyond donor identity, so even the primary donor contrast may combine donor, base, proton-management, and solvation effects. Inert-gas, no-cobalt, dark, and 13CO2 controls improve assignment but do not identify the exact active intermediate or prove a productive donor-catalyst adduct.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - E28 improves enough to move from revise to shortlist. The prior concern about missing inert-gas background bounding is now addressed by explicit inert-gas controls for both TEOA and TEA via G05 and G06, in addition to the no-cobalt and dark controls already added in the prior revision. This materially strengthens the core catalytic comparison by bounding donor-dependent non-CO2 gas background on both donor sides. The card also remains appropriately staged: it does not claim that the optional substrate-associated spectroscopic module is already established, and it limits conclusions if that module is not pilot-qualified. The quenching comparator is still pilot-defined rather than numerically preset, but the card operationalizes it through an empirically determined repeatability bound from replicate baseline measurements, which is adequate for researcher planning in an optional candidate. With the stronger control structure and bounded claims, shortlist status is now defensible.

E29 | Amine omission and TEA versus TEOA contrast under matched CO2 exposure

Question: Test whether donor presence is necessary for any CO2-associated spectroscopic signature and whether TEOA gives a donor-identity-specific signature or product-origin pattern beyond a generic amine effect in the P01 Co-bpy context.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: EV029 and GX011 motivate a transferable hypothesis that TEOA can participate in CO2- or catalyst-linked adduct chemistry rather than acting only as an electron donor. EV061 adds the alternative that amine effects may instead reflect quenching and CO2-solvation behavior. GX008 supports matched negative controls and 13CO2 origin checks. Proposed transfer is limited: these sources are not direct TEA-versus-TEOA evidence in the target Co-bpy system, so the experiment is framed as a discrimination test, not mechanism confirmation.

EV029, EV061, GX011, GX008

Supports: Support for H05 would be a reproducible CO2-associated spectroscopic feature or isotopically bounded behavior present with TEOA but absent in both TEA and no-amine groups, or substantially stronger with TEOA while upstream electron-supply observables remain similar in the matched comparison. Additional support would be TEOA-specific changes in CO/H2 output that track that signature across repeats.

Refutes: Evidence would weigh against H05 if TEA and TEOA show no meaningful difference in the targeted signatures or 13CO2-bounded behavior under matched conditions, and any catalytic differences are absent or better aligned with bulk medium changes or known quenching differences rather than donor-specific CO2-associated states. A complete loss of the original TEA/TEOA selectivity difference under stricter controls would also weaken the hypothesis.

Inconclusive: Results are inconclusive if signals are below detection, poorly assigned, not reproducible, or changed together with uncontrolled covariates such as amine-induced medium shifts. Similar product outputs with insufficient spectroscopic sensitivity, or donor-specific spectra without validated relation to catalysis, also remain non-discriminating.

Parameter selection: Do not invent concentrations, irradiation settings, or acquisition windows. Use the reported P01 formulation where explicitly supplied; otherwise verify from source records or run a pilot that first identifies a stable baseline with detectable CO and H2 and a measurable donor-sensitive spectral region. For TEA substitution, start with the same nominal donor role used in the baseline and document any bulk-property mismatch. If signal saturation or no signal occurs, adjust only the analytical acquisition settings or sample path length before changing chemistry. Use 13CO2 only after natural-abundance runs show sufficient CO signal for isotopic discrimination.

Limitations: TEA and TEOA differ simultaneously in donor, base, coordination, and medium effects, so even a single-factor formulation change does not isolate one microscopic cause. Cross-system literature on Re or other catalysts motivates but does not prove analogous Co-bpy adducts. No-amine controls may strongly suppress catalysis, limiting comparison to necessity rather than selectivity. Spectroscopic assignments may be ambiguous without orthogonal confirmation, and recovered-sample measurements may miss the operating state.

Feasibility: moderate

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - E29 remains a useful and appropriately bounded discrimination test. It compares TEOA, TEA, and no-amine conditions under matched P01 CO2 exposure, which directly addresses whether observed behavior is a generic amine effect, a donor-identity-specific effect, or absent without amine. The proposal explicitly documents bulk covariates rather than assuming TEA and TEOA are chemically equivalent, which is important because the substitution bundles multiple chemical changes. Optional 13CO2 use is framed correctly as an origin-bounding aid rather than mechanism proof. The evidence is transferred rather than direct for the target Co-bpy system, but that is acceptable because the candidate is framed as a discrimination screen rather than confirmation of a specific mechanistic species.

E30 | Symmetric cobalt-linked adduct screen

Question: Screen whether TEA versus TEOA under matched P01 conditions gives donor-specific operando substrate-associated signals that are cobalt-dependent and CO2-origin-bounded on both donor sides, while limiting conclusions to descriptive donor-linked differences unless orthogonal assignment succeeds.

Literature-to-proposal transfer: GX011 motivates a transferable possibility that TEOA can enter catalyst-associated adduct chemistry, but only in a Re system. EV004 and EV068 support operando state-sensitive measurements to compare catalyst-state changes with intermediate-related signals. EV085 warns that donor decomposition and altered active species can mimic catalyst-linked behavior, requiring no-cobalt and dark controls. EV080 and GX008 support isotopic and orthogonal assignment controls. This proposal transfers control logic and measurement strategy, not a proven Co mechanism.

GX011, EV004, EV068, EV085, EV080, GX008

Supports: Support for H05 would be a reproducible TEA/TEOA difference in operando substrate-associated observables under matched conditions, with at least one donor-specific feature showing cobalt dependence or donor-correlated cobalt-state change and isotope behavior consistent with CO2-derived origin on the relevant donor side.

Refutes: Evidence against H05 would be no reproducible donor-specific operando difference after matched controls, or donor-specific signals that are fully reproduced without cobalt and lack isotope support or cobalt-state correlation, making cobalt-linked or donor-specific CO2-adduct chemistry unnecessary.

Inconclusive: Inconclusive outcomes include unresolved spectral overlap, donor-dependent background absorption, failure of the chosen cobalt-sensitive method to resolve state differences, or isotope tests too weak for assignment. Such outcomes may still justify descriptive reporting of similarities or differences without mechanistic assignment.

Parameter selection: Do not assume spectral windows, concentrations, or irradiation settings from unrelated systems. Use source-verified P01 settings if available; otherwise run a pilot with identical cell geometry, gas delivery, and background subtraction across groups. Advance a cobalt-sensitive method only if it can be collected reproducibly for both donors under the same sampling configuration; otherwise restrict the card to IR-based descriptive screening.

Limitations: Direct Co-bpy evidence for TEA/TEOA adduct chemistry is absent in the supplied record. Heterogeneous background from g-C3N4 and mixed media may obscure weak bands. Even symmetric isotope controls do not cleanly distinguish amine-linked from cobalt-linked CO2-derived structures. No-cobalt controls alter the coordination environment, so they support but do not prove catalyst-linked assignment.

Feasibility: specialist

Review status: shortlist

API review: shortlist - E30 now merits shortlist because the prior asymmetry concern has been explicitly corrected: the card includes isotope assignment controls on both donor sides via G06 for TEOA and G08 for TEA, alongside no-cobalt and dark controls for both donor conditions. That makes donor-specific CO2-derived assignment symmetrically bounded at the design level. The spectroscopy package remains pilot-dependent, but the card now clearly states that if a cobalt-sensitive method is not reproducibly viable under the same operando configuration, the study should proceed as an IR-only descriptive screen with narrowed inference. This is acceptable for an optional specialist candidate because the intended conclusions are explicitly limited to donor dependence, cobalt dependence, light dependence, and isotope sensitivity rather than unique intermediate identity. Evidence support remains transferred from other systems and methods, not direct Co-bpy proof, but the control logic is adequate for the stated descriptive screening question.
